# Tasks: 001 Corporate Signing MVP

**Input**: [plan.md](plan.md) (runtime, runner contract, mode registry, governed invariants), [spec.md](spec.md), [data-model.md](data-model.md), [contracts/](contracts/), [research.md](research.md), [quickstart.md](quickstart.md).

Status: Phase 1 and Phase 2 are closed with receipts (`receipts/phase-2-close.md`), and their 13 tasks are marked [X]. US1's eight tasks (T006–T011, T034 and T035) are accepted and marked [X], and the Phase 3 checkpoint is closed with its receipt (`receipts/phase-3-checkpoint.md`), so US2's commits may start. In US2, T012, T013, T036 and T039 are accepted and marked [X] (requests #41, #42, #44 and #45). Every other task stays unchecked until its evidence passes. This file is the only executable build checklist; the OpenSpec tasks hold governance and handoff only.

**Conventions.**
- `files:` paths are relative to the paired code worktree.
- Every `verify:` runs from the code tree with `PATH="$PWD/tools/node/bin:$PATH"`, after `sh scripts/fetch-node.sh` and `npm ci`.
- Every mode used below is pre-registered by T001 in `scripts/verify-modes.mjs` (plan.md § Mode registry).
- Tier tags follow the shared rubric. 40 tasks are `[O]` (security-sensitive, cross-module, contract or governed paths) and 1 is `[S]`. No `[H]` task qualifies.

**Orchestrator checks for every task after T001.**
1. **Freeze.** `git diff --quiet <T001-sha> HEAD -- scripts/verify.mjs scripts/verify-modes.mjs tests/tooling/verification-runner.test.mjs`, where `<T001-sha>` is the current governed runner commit: amendment A-007 in plan.md, which supersedes the A-006 baseline. The runner, its registry and its self-test are frozen there, and no later task lists them.
2. **Scope.** The task's commit touches only its `files:`.
3. **Verify.** The task's `verify:` exits 0 and its final line reads `PASS`.
4. **Regression.** `npm run verify -- no-secrets` and every earlier task's mode still pass.

**How runner statuses are handled.**
- `CAPABILITY_ABSENT` (exit 3) is recorded as **environment-blocked**. It is never a pass, and it never climbs the model-escalation ladder.
- `VACUOUS`, `EVIDENCE_MISSING` and `ENV_MISMATCH` are failures.

**Writer brief hygiene** (risk report § C):
- Engineering language only.
- Synthetic fixtures are generated programmatically.
- Writers return summaries only: case IDs, counts and pass/fail tables, never key, token or fixture bytes.
- The T012 oracle writer gets a fresh context. Its brief excludes `src/cryptograph/` and `src/verifier/`, and T013/T036/T037/T014 writers never edit `tests/integrity/oracle/`.

A-010 (R4, from the re-check of ad05ba5; fixed in e1be709). A placeholder's default or alternate value is scanned, never exempt (NS-11). Backtick template literals are scanned (NS-12). - [X] T002 [O] Build the license inventory and SBOM tool. It classifies every npm package as `shipped` (production closure plus any asset copied into `dist/`) or `non-shipped` (devDependencies). It evaluates SPDX expressions against `scripts/license-policy.json`, which allows exactly `MIT` and `Apache-2.0`. An OR passes only when one alternative is fully allowed; an AND needs every term allowed. Missing or unknown licenses fail, and so does any extra `LICENSE*`/`NOTICE*`/`COPYING*` file inside a shipped package. It emits CycloneDX JSON to `.verify-out/`, regenerates `NOTICE`, and writes the separate platform inventory with status `INVENTORIED`: pinned Node, `ldd` of the binary, OpenSSL CLI, util-linux `unshare`, Chromium revision, Hermes image digest. Platforms are recorded separately under the owner decision and never affect the application gate. The browsers path is resolved exactly as the frozen runner does: `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH` when set, otherwise the default cache. Tests build synthetic `node_modules` trees at runtime: 0BSD, `(MIT AND Zlib)`, `BSD-3-Clause OR GPL-2.0` and BlueOak fail; MIT, Apache-2.0 and `MIT OR Apache-2.0` pass; a missing license fails; an extra asset license in a shipped package fails; a disallowed dev-only package is reported as non-shipped; LI-09 asserts platform status `INVENTORIED` and that platform entries never affect the application gate (LI-01..LI-10). A-009 follow-up, before T025: the platform inventory adds the parse-sandbox launchers: `bwrap` (Debian `bubblewrap`), `unshare`/`setpriv` (`util-linux`), `chroot` (`coreutils`) and, under A-010, `/usr/bin/mount` and `/bin/sh`, which the `unshare`+`chroot`+`setpriv` launcher runs. Each tool path is resolved through its symlinks to the real file, and its owning package comes from the package manager. A tool with no package record is reported as such, with no license filled in. Licenses come only from the package records (as recorded on the bench: bubblewrap LGPL-2+, util-linux GPL-2+, coreutils GPL-3+), and no license is invented or relaxed (LI-11). — files: `scripts/license-inventory.mjs`, `scripts/license-policy.json`, `scripts/platform-inventory.mjs`, `tests/license-inventory/`, `NOTICE`, `docs/qualification.md`, `docs/platform-inventory.md` — verify: `npm run verify -- license-inventory`
- [X] T027 [O] Land and qualify the complete third-party package set with exact pins. It adds playwright pinned at 1.61.1, whose browser is Chromium revision 1228. That revision must be present at the effective browsers path: `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH` when set, otherwise the default cache. It confirms the shipped closure is empty and records the non-shipped inventory: typescript's and playwright-core's third-party notices. No later task may add a package. Tests: QL-01 shipped gate PASS; QL-02 every package.json version is exact; QL-03 the "dev bytes not in distributable" check fails on a synthetic dist that contains dev-package bytes (QL-03 fingerprints output bytes only; on its own it does not prove non-contribution, which T035's build provenance does); QL-04 browser revision matches; QL-05 install works with `ignore-scripts`. The qualification run writes the registry evidence `.verify-out/qualification/inventory.json` — files: `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `tests/qualification/`, `docs/qualification.md` — verify: `npm run verify -- qualification`

In the current round, as a test-only item, add a Linux-only regression test with a real non-dumpable live writer (an execute-only copy of the pinned node) holding a torn, aged lock. It asserts that `--break-lock damaged` refuses, names the writer as uninspectable, and leaves the lock untouched, and that the writer then completes and releases. The test first proves its premise, that a peer cannot read the writer's `/proc/<pid>/fd`. Off Linux, or where the premise cannot be set up (for example when run as root), it skips with the reason, so the mode is VACUOUS (exit 4) and never a pass (OR-41). A case that must move the lock gives the library only pids the test owns and keeps alive, through the existing `pids` seam. A host-wide scan appears only in assertions that hold under any host state; the whole-`/proc` CLI run ends either in the move or in a named refusal with the lock untouched. The uninspectable-peer refusal stays tested and unchanged (OR-41, and OR-35 (d)); the CLI keeps scanning the whole of `/proc` and gains no pid override. Proof on a scratch copy: a scratch-only injected peer that turns uninspectable after the oracle's last scan reproduces the 7a02449 signature at base, and the amended test passes with it present; a mutant without the uninspectable refusal fails. `src/storage/backup/` and `scripts/backup.mjs` stay untouched; if a product defect is proved, stop and report (OR-35, amended). It passes that path, and removes the directory when it exits (data-model.md § Keys and secrets). - [X] T031 [O] Implement the HTTP server entrypoint and router. Router: the route registry `src/api/routes.ts`, a loopback default bind, and an optional Unix-domain-socket listener for the isolated Hermes relay. Responses and limits: streaming body limits (JSON 256 KiB, PDF 10 MiB → 413 before a full read), uniform `Error` bodies with correlation IDs, and an Origin allowlist. Security headers: CSP with no inline or eval, `frame-ancestors 'none'` (only the recipient document response allows `'self'`), `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `nosniff`. Logging: a central log redactor for capability values, cookies, `Authorization`, webhook secrets and PEM blocks. Tests SEC-01..SEC-06 assert headers and grep captured logs. A-008 follow-up for P11, run in the main worktree after the lanes merge and before US1: wire the storage-backed TenantPolicy, and refuse to start when deployment configuration and the tenants table disagree (SEC-07). A-009 follow-up, in the same session as SEC-07. LBR-2: once a `Connection: close` response is decided, no later pipelined request is parsed or dispatched (SEC-08). LBR-6: startup logs the authenticator's policy state, never secrets (SEC-09). A-010 (LBR4-1), same session: request bodies get an idle timeout of at most 10 s and a minimum-rate rule. Both are enforced by a per-request timer, not Node's 30 s check interval, and answered with 408 and a connection close (SEC-10). A-010 (LBR5-1, from the lane B round-5 re-check), in a T031 follow-up slot just before T034: while a response is pending on a connection that will close, extra bytes are never answered with the parser's 400. Pipelined bytes after a mutating `Connection: close` request, or after a complete upload body, get the pending response first and then the connection closes (SEC-11). A-010 (row WIRE-US1), US1 composition slot after T010 and before T035. Set `ROUTES` from the handler maps that T008, T034 and T010 export (`withRecipientHandlers()` and its peers). Compose their dependencies in `src/server/main.ts`: the database, the blob store, the T032 notifier and the challenge key file `OPENSIG_CHALLENGE_KEY_FILE`, which startup refuses unless it is a regular file, not a symbolic link, owned by the server user, with no group or other permission bit (for example 0600 or 0400), and holding 32 to 4096 bytes (request #30). Amend SEC-04 and SEC-05 without weakening them (§ Rulings). SEC-07's readEnvelope assertion is amended the same way: its 501 becomes the handler's 404 `not_found`, shown to come from the handler (request #30). SEC-12 proves that the composed server serves every wired route through its real handler and refuses to start without a valid key file. Every listener shares T034's process-wide per-tenant upload cap, and the composition states this (request #16). The same slot carries SEC-13 and SEC-14 (rows SEC11-D5, SEC11-Q, SEC11-D6). SEC-13 covers three cases: (a) a stalled partial request behind a pending response never displaces it, so the pending response goes first and then the 408 or close; (b) a client half-close while a response is pending still gets that response; (c) a malformed chunk that arrives after a pipelined request's handler was entered gets the uniform 400 `Error`, with its correlation ID, right after the pending response, and then the connection closes. It never waits 10 s for the idle timer, and its body-read permit is released at once (request #30). Node cannot keep a half-closed socket without the undocumented `httpAllowHalfOpen`, so case (b) is a documented T024 limitation instead, and the optional patch is not applied (request #30). SEC-14 fails loudly if the Node internal `socket._httpMessage._headerSent`, which the SEC-11 replay reads, is missing or changes type. A-010 (rows T008FU-D5 and T008FU-D9), same slot: SEC-15 proves on the composed server, with a blocked notifier, that the generic 202 is written to the socket before any background ceremony step starts. SEC-16 proves that shutdown calls `ceremony.drain()` and waits for pending background jobs, so none is dropped silently. A-010 (request #30), same slot. Shutdown waits for every dispatched request handler to settle, then drains the ceremony, then stops the expiry sweeper, and only then closes the database; SEC-16 shows the order (row SHUTDOWN-INFLIGHT). The composition starts T010's `ExpirySweeper` once, over the composed database and the ceremony's `onEnvelopeChange`, at its default interval; SEC-12 shows it (row SWEEP-SCHED). The `closed` line carries the parse service's `parseOrphans()` and `parseStopsPastCap()` totals, and a warning line is logged when either has risen since it was last read, checked at least once a minute; SEC-16 shows both (row PARSE-COUNTS). `requestTimeout` is computed from the SEC-10 constants and is at least the longest request SEC-10 admits: `headersTimeout` plus the PDF limit at the minimum rate plus the storage busy timeout for a transaction that runs before the body read (665 s with today's constants). Every term is read from its owner's export and none is restated. - [X] T033 [O] Implement the bounded PDF parser and the isolated parse service. Parser handles: xref tables and streams, object streams, the page tree with inherited `/Rotate` and `/MediaBox`, and detection of encryption, existing signatures, JavaScript/Launch/EmbeddedFile/RichMedia. Parser limits: nesting depth, object count, decompression ratio and total inflated bytes. Service: a child process with memory, CPU and wall-time limits, and the Node permission model with no fs-write, child-process or worker grants. Upload validation and composition both use it. Network isolation: when the service's own probe succeeds, the worker is launched inside a new user and network namespace with util-linux `unshare` (`--user --net`, mapping `--map-current-user` where supported, otherwise `--map-root-user`; no host privilege and no privileged host change). When the capability is unavailable, the service fails closed in production mode, which is the default. In development mode only (explicitly configured), it runs unsandboxed and reports `network_sandbox: unavailable` in its result. Every parse result reports the observed capability as `netns+fs-enforced (local)` or `unavailable`, and never claims the production gate `parser-network-sandbox` passed. Tests PL-01..PL-12, including a 10 MiB streaming cap, a 51-page rejection, rotated-page geometry, resource-limit kills and the import-boundary check that `src/pdf/` imports no `net`/`http`/`https`/`dns`/`tls`/`dgram`. That check (PL-12 as implemented) is relabelled "import boundary (defence in depth), not network isolation". Extra tagged test PL-13, outside the frozen required-case list: a probe launched through the service's sandbox launcher fails to connect to a parent-side loopback listener at the OS level, an unsandboxed control connects, and the sandboxed worker still parses. Where the namespace is unavailable, PL-13 instead asserts the production fail-closed path and the development `unavailable` label, and the writer's report states which branch ran. A-008 rework. S2: reject an effective CropBox that differs from the MediaBox with 422, detail `cropbox_differs` (PL-14). P15: run unsandboxed only with `OPENSIG_PARSER_ALLOW_UNSANDBOXED=1` and only when namespaces are unavailable, never because of OPENSIG_ENV or NODE_ENV (PL-15). P08: treat `/XFA`, `/OnInstantiate`, `/Subtype /3D`, `/Subtype /Screen` and `/S /Rendition` as active content (PL-16). P09: the parent validates the child's report: pages 1..50, finite positive sizes, the rotate enum, and a byte count equal to the input (PL-17). A-009 rework. LBR-1: isolate filesystem IPC as well as the network. Launch the worker with a mount namespace and a minimal read-only root (tmpfs plus read-only binds of only the Node runtime, the library directories it needs and the worker code), with no capabilities, and with input over the stdio pipe. Use whichever launcher the runtime probe finds: bubblewrap (preferred: `bwrap --unshare-all --die-with-parent --new-session --cap-drop ALL`), or the util-linux composition (`unshare --user --net --mount` plus `chroot` plus `setpriv`). Both are platform tools, inventoried separately and never application dependencies. When neither is usable, production returns 500 `internal`/`sandbox-unavailable`. A pathname AF_UNIX connect, or write access to any host socket, must fail from inside the sandbox. Any missing piece means production fails closed. The label becomes `netns+fs-enforced (local)`, and the overstated "any connect fails" comment is corrected (PL-18). LBR-3: take the test seams off the public `parsePdf` options, and make the probe confirm isolation from inside the sandbox rather than trusting any exit 0 (PL-19). LBR-4: bound and shape-check `objects`, `trailer` and page `ref` in the parent (PL-20). LBR-5, due before T034 is accepted: reject `/S /SubmitForm`, `/ImportData`, `/GoToR`, `/GoToE`, `/Subtype /Movie` and `/Sound`, and any `/OpenAction` or `/AA` action other than same-document navigation. Plain `/URI` link annotations stay allowed (PL-21). A-010 (LBR2, from the lane B re-check round 2 of 2c942bf). Honest documents with a dangling reference at or beyond `/Size`, an `/ID` longer than 256 bytes or a name longer than 1024 characters pass the parent's LBR-4 bounds and parse as before, never 500 `service-failure` (PL-22). Restore the original assertions dropped from PL-13 and PL-15. No launcher leaves a writable `/dev` inside the sandbox; bubblewrap's `--dev /dev` tmpfs is made read-only (PL-23). A-010 (LBR3-1, from the lane B round-3 re-check of dcb8fce): bound the parent's cost of decoding a child report. The child reports only the fields that upload validation and the composer use. `maxReportBytes` and a node budget are derived from the parser's own limits, and the parent enforces both while converting the report; an over-budget report fails closed with 500 `service-failure`. Concurrent parses are capped by a constant in `src/pdf/service.ts`. A maximal lying report adds at most 128 MiB to the parent heap, a quarter of the child's 512 MiB cap, and honest documents still parse (PL-22). The event-loop block for a maximal report is measured and recorded in the report (PL-24). A-010 (LBR4-1, from the lane B round-4 re-check of d037f99), in the post-merge main-worktree follow-up: take a parse slot only after the input is fully buffered within the 10 MiB cap, so a slow body never holds a parse slot and composition never waits behind uploads. Body reads have their own small concurrency cap, separate from parse slots, and a client abort leaves no slot held (PL-25). A-010 (row PL24-ORC), test-oracle fix. PL-24 keeps every cap, capacity and refusal assertion: at most 2 running, 32 waiting, then a 429 `parse-capacity` at once without reading the body. Its completion-order assertions are replaced by deterministic admission evidence from the gate itself, a read-only and bounded admission record exported beside `parseCapacity()` that gives each acquisition an arrival ticket. The test issues each call only after `waiting` grows, then asserts that the first two admitted are `slow-0` and `slow-1` and that the waiting parses are admitted in exact arrival order 0..31. Completion order is never asserted, and child start stamps are not admission evidence. There is no production behaviour change (PL-24, amended). A-010 (row PL25-ORC), test-only slot right after the corrected SEC-11 push and before the T008 follow-up. PL-25 keeps every cap, capacity and refusal assertion, and its oracle becomes deterministic. Barriers on permits held (2, then 4) replace the sleeps. With the slow uploads in flight, nothing parses or waits, and `parseAdmissions().next_ticket` has not moved. The honest upload and the composition both enter the gate and are admitted with nothing waiting. Their 200s are awaited under a hang guard that fails with its own message, and latency is logged as a diagnostic only. The refused upload sends headers only and gets 429 before any body byte, and `pulled === 0` holds for the direct stream. Test 1's cleanup is unconditional (destroy, await, drain to idle), and test 2 starts by asserting idle. `src/pdf/` stays untouched. The original slow phase was never observed, so if the hang guard fires or a product defect is proved, stop and report (PL-25, amended). A-010 (row PL11-WALL, request #8), a test-only slot right after the T008 follow-up push and before the T007 slot, changing only `tests/pdf-parse/isolation.test.mjs`. Line 64 stays on the real parser. Line 65's 5 s bound on the whole call, which also timed the test's own `/proc` scan, is replaced. The measurement now stops when the parse call resolves, and the bound becomes `DEFAULT_SERVICE_LIMITS.wallMs`, read rather than copied, after asserting that it is at least 10 times the configured 300 ms. The message carries the elapsed time. This shows that the configured wall limit, not the default, ended the child. A new PL-11 case uses `createParseServiceForTests({ workerEntry })` to run a worker that blocks forever without using CPU, at `wallMs: 300`. Its `wall-time` outcome, with no worker left, proves the kill reached the real child, and the same bound applies. A hang guard above that bound fails loudly with its own message; it is never a pass criterion, and no claim rests on it. Phase timings are diagnostics. Proof on a scratch copy: a mutant whose child outlives the kill fails, a mutant that ignores the configured wall limit fails, and the unmodified tree passes. Recorded limit: PL-11 does not prove termination within any fixed latency after 300 ms under arbitrary host load. Resource limits, `src/pdf/` and every other timing assertion stay untouched (PL-11, amended). A-010 (rows KILL-RSS and KILL-CPU, LOW), with any T033 product slot that PL11-SLOW causes, otherwise a test-only touch before T025. Two cases follow the blocking-worker pattern, each with a hang guard and self-exit containment. KILL-RSS comes first: a worker holds resident memory above `rssBytes`, outside the V8 heap, and parks without exiting. It expects `memory` and no surviving node process of its worker, so a missed RSS trigger (which gives `wall-time`) and a missed signal (which hangs) both fail. KILL-CPU: a worker burns CPU without ever exiting and must end with `cpu` and no survivor. This proves the CPU limit ends the child, by the parent's kill or the kernel RLIMIT_CPU backstop, and the test claims neither one specifically. The V8 heap case (`heapMiB`) is self-enforced and is not part of this gap. The KILL cases change only tests, and the limits stay untouched (PL-11, amended). A-010 (row PL11-ESC, request #12), a product slot before T034, within T033's files: when the service decides a kill, the kill ends the service's own whole sandbox, owned process tree or namespace, including any process that starts after the decision. The wall budget still runs from spawn, as now, and covers sandbox start-up; arming the watchdog only once the child is ready is not an acceptable fix on its own. The writer chooses the mechanism. It acts only on the service's own sandbox tree or namespace, with no global scan or kill, no limit weakened and no host load generated. Tests-first proof (PL-26, new): a test deliberately kills during sandbox start-up, through a test seam inside T033's files if needed. It asserts that the whole owned tree or namespace has ended, that no output arrives after the kill, and that the outcome settles only once the sandbox is gone. It fails on 789a3f7 and passes after the fix. If no deterministic start-up kill can be built, stop and report. KILL-RSS and KILL-CPU ride in this slot (PL-26, PL-11 amended). Request #14 accepts the stop-capture-kill design for Phase B proof. On a decided kill, the launcher is stopped, its children are captured and SIGKILLed through captured identity, and then the launcher is SIGKILLed. Settlement waits for stdio to close and for every captured init to end. Spawn options stay unchanged, with no `detached` launch and no group signal. The orphan path ends the captured children before settling and keeps the existing 422 `resource-limit`/`cpu` mapping; no outcome settles while an owned member can still run. The service exposes a process-wide, read-only `orphans_ended` count beside `parseCapacity()`, in the same pattern, with no logger in `src/pdf/`. The orphan test asserts that the count increments, and the test service's lifecycle record stays the test evidence (request #15). `orphans_ended` counts the captured children that the service actually ended through their captured identity after their launcher's exit was seen, benign cleanup of an init whose death signal was already pending included (O1; request #17). The orphan test still proves a counted orphan, 1, from its held `/bin/sh` launcher, whose child has no death signal armed. Phase B also carries a service-death check for both launchers, and a structure case per usable launcher that turns Q3 from inference into proof. Under bwrap, the structure case asserts the observed layout: the launcher is the service's own child, its only child is the init, the init is pid 1 of its namespace and leads its own session and group, and the worker is the init's child. The normal-run check, under BOTH launchers, asserts properties instead of a zero count (request #17): the normal outcome, unchanged; no limit-decided kill (kill null); every captured child terminated by settlement, as D2 settles, meaning reaped, or a zombie whose `Threads:` count is 1, read through the captured `/proc` handle (request #20); and reaped within one default wall period of the channel closing, read from `DEFAULT_SERVICE_LIMITS` (request #18); nothing of the owned tree or namespace runnable after settlement, checked through captured identities and their task entries rather than command lines (request #20); the per-child count equals the number of captured children the service ended, as the lifecycle record shows. If fd 3 cannot reach the worker under a launcher, stop and report. Every test seam is reachable only through `createParseServiceForTests`, and `parsePdf` refuses it (LBR-3). The residual windows are recorded as row PL11-ESC-LIM. A-010 (request #20), a follow-up commit in this slot. (i) A captured child counts as terminated only if it is reaped, or is a zombie whose `Threads:` count is 1, read through its captured `/proc` handle. Settlement waits for that on the existing poll tick, and the test bounds the wait below one default wall period. Check (d) inspects captured identities and their task entries, not command lines. (ii) The launcher is SIGKILLed only once it is observed stopped, or has already exited. While a stop is pending past the cap, every poll tick re-captures the launcher's children through ownership and SIGKILLs each through captured identity, so a child forked late, or by a fork in flight, is ended. A launcher that stays alive keeps its children capturable, and a stopped launcher cannot fork. A deterministic test proves it through a test-only hold reachable only via `createParseServiceForTests`: the launcher is kept from stopping within the cap while it forks a late child. The test asserts the late child is ended through its identity, the launcher is killed only after it is observed stopped, and nothing is runnable after settlement. A mutant that kills only the earlier snapshot must fail. A-010 (request #45), one T033 commit in the T036 follow-up slot, made by that slot's writer before its T036 commit. When the AcroForm's `/SigFlags` is an indirect reference to a non-negative integer object, the report carries that object, its integer value only, and no other new object. A dictionary, array, stream, negative or dangling target is not reported, and such a document parses exactly as before. The service's object-count cap becomes 4 + 2 × pages. A parser-limits test (PL-27, an extra case) shows the integer target reported, each other target not reported with the document still parsed, the new cap, and the report's top-level keys unchanged. General object reporting is not expanded, and T033 keeps [X]. — files: `src/pdf/`, `tests/pdf-parse/` — verify: `npm run verify -- parser-limits`

## Phase 3: User Story 1 — Review and approve a corporate document (P1)

**Goal:** a synthetic one- or two-recipient envelope reaches "fully approved" (`approvals_complete=true`, state `finalizing`), visibly short of completed and sealed.

**Independent test:** `us1`. A second recipient cannot approve first; neither sender nor service can approve; opening the link never approves.

An envelope gathers at most `ENVELOPE_REPEATABLE_EVENT_CAP` = 2048 audit events through the actions a person can repeat: invitation resends, challenge requests and recipient-session creation. Each of these counts the envelope's audit events through the `audit_events_envelope` index, inside its own write transaction, and refuses once the count has reached the cap, writing nothing. The refusal is 429 `rate_limited` with detail `envelope_event_limit`, which every one of these operations already declares. The cap is durable, unlike the per-process rate limits, and it is exported for T016's receipt budget. Only the repeatable actions are refused; an approval, a decline, a void, an expiry and every finalization event are never refused for the cap. — files: `src/api/recipients/`, `src/api/auth/recipient-session.ts`, `src/domain/consent/`, `src/storage/repositories/recipients.ts`, `src/storage/schema/`, `src/api/routes.ts`, `tests/recipients/` — verify: `npm run verify -- recipients`

## Phase 4: User Story 2 — Obtain and verify a protected package (P1)

**Goal:** from "fully approved", compose, seal, independently verify, durably store and publish exactly one package.

**Independent test:** `us2-local` produces ten verified flows. Untrusted certificates are labeled untrusted, and tampering is detected.

- [X] T012 [US2] [O] Build the independent test oracle (fresh writer context). It assembles hand-built minimal PDFs, signs them with `openssl cms -sign` using development certificates generated at runtime in an owner-only `mkdtemp` directory (mode 0700), with private key files at 0600, runtime-only and removed after use (request #39; OpenSSL 3.0.13 is present on the bench, per the driver's preflight of 2026-11-18), and implements its own ByteRange and revision structure checker. It uses `openssl cms -verify -binary -inform DER` over the ByteRange bytes. It imports nothing from `src/`. `fixtures/synthetic/integrity/` holds only structural descriptors and expected outcomes. Kind `harness`. Status: non-shipped test tool (OpenSSL CLI listed in the platform inventory). Tests OC-01..OC-08. A-008 (S1): the oracle reads the `opensig-approval-evidence-digest` signed attribute independently of the product (OC-09). A-010 (request #39, Part E): OC-01..OC-09 are defined in plan.md § Case catalogs (OC). OC-09's CMS may be built by inserting the attribute with the oracle's own DER code and re-signing the new signedAttrs SET with `openssl dgst -sha256 -sign`, kept only if `openssl cms -verify` passes. A negative control shows that the spliced CMS with the old signature fails, and a failed build stops the run; it never skips the case. The verdict mapping that T014 must agree with: integrity is `valid` only when the ByteRange, CMS-structure and messageDigest checks and OpenSSL's verification over the stated ranges all pass, and otherwise `invalid`; the oracle never emits `indeterminate` integrity. Trust is `indeterminate` unless integrity is `valid`, and otherwise `trusted` or `untrusted` against only the case's anchors, with the default CA file, directory and store disabled and `-purpose any`. revision_coverage is purely structural: `complete` when the structure and ByteRange checks pass and the signed revision is the last, `incomplete` when it is not the last, and `indeterminate` otherwise. Acceptance as the original needs integrity `valid`, coverage `complete`, `artifact_match` true and the approval evidence `match` or not requested; it ignores trust. Every corpus case supplies the recorded artifact digest. The structure checker reads classic xref tables, cross-reference streams (uncompressed, and FlateDecode with the PNG predictors) and hybrid files (a table whose trailer carries `/XRefStm`, which T033's parser accepts), enough to walk the `/Prev` chain and check every type-1 entry. A type-2 entry must name an object stream that a type-1 entry locates. A limit is an inclusive maximum: the defaults accept 65,534 entries and 0xFFFFFFFE bytes, and a lowered limit accepts its own value and refuses one more (request #44); a caller may lower them, never raise them, and T016 passes the product's bounds. Every header uses version needed 2.0, version made by Unix 2.0, the external attributes of a regular file with mode 0644 and internal attributes 0, and the UTF-8 name flag is always set. ## Phase 5: User Story 3 — Hermes and shared consumer contracts (P2)

**Goal:** real Hermes v0.23.0 tool dispatch completes a synthetic, human-approved flow, and the healthcare harnesses enforce source-version binding.

**Independent test:** `us3`, isolated local Hermes with a scripted model. `us3-real-model` is a supplementary run.

- [ ] T018 [US3] [O] Implement the Hermes integration. `src/integrations/hermes/mcp-server.mjs`: a dependency-free stdio JSON-RPC MCP server that runs on Node ≥ 24, including the Hermes image's Node. It advertises exactly `prepare_envelope`, `request_signatures`, `get_status` and `retrieve_completed_package`, with no approve, decline, session, confirmation, callback, void, retry or key tool. It relays to the openSig API using a synthetic service credential. The adapter validates the preview digests. `request_signatures` requires an `authorization_reference`, and no tool result contains a capability value. Kind `contract`. Tests HM-01..HM-06 — files: `src/integrations/hermes/`, `tests/integrations/hermes/` — verify: `npm run verify -- hermes-contract`
- [ ] T019 [US3] [O] Implement webhooks. Registration and key rotation/revocation handlers, for human tenant administrators only. Destination policy: HTTPS plus an allowlist. Private, loopback, link-local, metadata, IPv4-mapped and alternate-form addresses are rejected at registration and at every connection: resolve once, validate every A/AAAA record, connect to that address with SNI, follow no redirects, ignore proxy environment variables. Tests use an injected resolver and connector only. Signing: HMAC-SHA256 over `<timestamp>.<raw body>` with the key ID header and overlap rotation. The secret is shown once and encrypted at rest. Outbox delivery keeps a stable `event_id`. Tests WH-01..WH-08. A-008 obligation (P10): an appended migration adds per-registration `webhook_deliveries` and tenant destination-allowlist storage, so one failing registration neither triggers resends to the others nor causes them to be abandoned (WH-09, WH-10). A-010: webhook-key issuance writes its idempotency record in withheld mode, with no body. Rule and test its replay status, and never re-reveal the secret; envelope routes refuse a withheld record with 500 (WH-11). A-010 (request #19): T019 owns the live cross-tenant 404 check for webhook-id routes. Once their handlers are installed, T034's SC002-26 compares only absent ids for them. — files: `src/integrations/webhooks/`, `src/api/webhooks/`, `src/storage/repositories/webhooks.ts`, `src/storage/schema/`, `src/api/routes.ts`, `tests/webhooks/` — verify: `npm run verify -- webhooks`
- [ ] T020 [US3] [O] Implement the synthetic openChart and openPractice harness consumers. Each fetches canonical status with its own credential, checks tenant mapping and source version, treats a duplicate as a no-op and rejects stale versions. Neither exposes any clinical-attestation or financial-action surface. Kind `contract`. Tests HC-01..HC-04 — files: `src/integrations/healthcare/`, `tests/integrations/healthcare/` — verify: `npm run verify -- healthcare-contracts`
- [ ] T021 [US3] [O] Implement the disabled and fake anchoring adapters and wire `anchor_status`. A fake failure never alters completion. A preloaded network guard on `net`, `dns`, `http` and `fetch` logs every connection attempt, and the log must be empty. Tests AN-01..AN-03 — files: `src/anchoring/`, `src/domain/envelopes/anchor-status.ts`, `tests/anchoring/` — verify: `npm run verify -- anchoring-disabled`
- [ ] T040 [US3] [O] Provision the isolated local Hermes harness. Image: the exact image `docker.io/nousresearch/hermes-agent@sha256:adb7890916de63245523492be8f68f9d2095fecbec4e2a6dab7960381f0fc002`, never pulled. Container: run with `--init --network none` and a tmpfs Hermes home. The config is generated per run from a template, with `mcp_servers.opensig` and `tools.include` set to exactly the four tools. Model and transport: a dependency-free scripted OpenAI-compatible endpoint runs inside the container. The MCP relay reaches the host openSig over a mounted Unix socket. Guards: a preflight refuses non-loopback or production endpoints, any `kubectl` and any production config path. Probe: `scripts/capabilities/hermes-runtime.mjs` exits 0 only when the Docker daemon and that local image are present. Tests HR-01..HR-03 — files: `fixtures/hermes/`, `scripts/hermes-local.mjs`, `scripts/capabilities/hermes-runtime.mjs`, `tests/hermes-runtime/` — verify: `npm run verify -- hermes-runtime`
- [ ] T022 [US3] [O] Demonstrate SC-006 through the isolated local Hermes. Run: a scripted-deterministic model drives prepare → (human `confirmSend`) → request → status → retrieve, in both `tool_search` modes. Recipients approve in Chromium through the recipient session, and a Hermes approval attempt is refused. Both healthcare harnesses run, as do disabled and fake-failing anchoring. Production Hermes is never contacted. Without the capability the run reports CAPABILITY_ABSENT. ## Phase 6: Release and cross-cutting

- [ ] T023 [O] Wire the code-leg CI as `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, never `validate.yml`, which is digest-pinned in the assembly. Actions are pinned by full SHA, and their licenses are inventoried. The pinned runtime comes from `scripts/fetch-node.sh`. Jobs run `npm ci`, `all-unit`, `no-secrets`, `license-inventory` and the openssl/chromium modes. The CI profile lists the excluded capability modes by name. The test forbids `continue-on-error`, `|| true`, `pull_request_target` and any `secrets.` use. The receipt writer binds results to the tree digest. Real CI evidence stays OPEN until a PR run. Tests RG-01..RG-06 — files: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `scripts/release-evidence.mjs`, `tests/release-gates/` — verify: `npm run verify -- release-gates`
- [ ] T024 [O] Document the pilot prerequisites and rollback, with no deployment. Documents: managed key custody, retention, backup/restore, and a threat model written as a table of assets, boundaries and controls mapped to registered case IDs. Tests: OP-01 runs a backup-restore drill, OP-02 detects a corrupted blob on restore, and OP-03 checks that every control maps to a registered case ID. A-009 (operator runbook): document `--first-backup`, `--first-restore`, `--complete-staged`, `--discard-staged`, `--break-lock` and `--retire-tenant`, every refusal code, and the single-host local-filesystem and lock assumptions (OP-04). A-010: also `--abandon-committed`, `--resync-ledger` and `--break-lock damaged`, the statuses with exit codes 74 and 75, and a crash during a first restore, which leaves a restored database, no ledger and the lock held (OP-04). A-010: list the no-secrets gate's documented limitations under its control in the threat model (§ Review findings ledger, no-secrets closure boundary; row NS-LIM). Also document the settings `OPENSIG_DATA_DIR` and `OPENSIG_DATABASE` (quickstart.md § Configuration), `lock-lost` (exit 70), and manual recovery of a damaged lock. The known limitation is that the break always refuses on a host with a same-user non-dumpable process (such as ssh-agent), a zombie, or a session under another primary gid (OP-04). A-010: also document `OPENSIG_CHALLENGE_KEY_FILE` (OP-04). A-010 (request #30): also document these (OP-04). The key file's rule is no group or other permission bit, and its directory must be writable only by the server user (row KEY-FIFO). The blob store is `$OPENSIG_DATA_DIR/blobs`, the directory that backup's `--blobs` names. The shutdown bound: in-flight handlers and pending ceremony jobs each end within their own deadlines (the storage busy timeout of 5 s per transaction, the SMTP session deadline of 10 s, the parse wall budget); the spool transport has no deadline and relies on the local-filesystem assumption; a second signal ends the process at once. SEC-13 (b)'s limitation: a client that half-closes while a response is pending may lose that response (row SEC11-D5). A write lock held by another process on the live database stalls the whole process for up to the busy timeout per write. An expiry sweep run is synchronous, so a backlog after downtime holds the event loop for that run (row SWEEP-SCHED). A-010 (request #32): also document that a request pipelined behind others on one connection waits for them, and that wait counts against Node's request timeout, so a client should not pipeline an upload (row PIPE-WAIT); and that startup settles the parse plan, which takes up to 10 s per launcher kind when a sandbox probe hangs (OP-04). A-010 (request #39): also document `OPENSIG_SEAL_KEY_FILE` and `OPENSIG_SEAL_CERT_FILE` (OP-04). A-010 (request #44): also document `OPENSIG_TRUST_ANCHORS_FILE`, the sealed-PDF bound of 48 MiB with its upload refusal, and that a sealed PDF and its package share T028's 64 MiB per-blob limit (OP-04). — files: `docs/operations.md`, `docs/threat-model.md`, `tests/operations/` — verify: `npm run verify -- operations`
- [ ] T025 [O] Execute local synthetic acceptance and commit the tree-bound receipt. The run covers every local component mode, and the SC case union must be complete. The receipt records `release_ready: false` and lists as OPEN: the external PDF reader, declared hardware, the CI run, the trusted certificate and key custody, real OIDC/SMTP, production Hermes and the parser network sandbox in the deployment environment (`parser-network-sandbox`). It reports the observed local parser network capability (`netns+fs-enforced (local)` or `unavailable`) and never states or implies that the production sandbox gate passed. The platform inventory is recorded separately and is not a gate. Any absent local capability yields CAPABILITY_ABSENT (environment-blocked). A-010 (row T30R41-N2): the receipt claims the CLI's `--break-lock damaged` move only from a run in which the CLI itself moved a lock; otherwise it records that path as not exercised locally — files: `docs/evidence/acceptance.json`, `docs/evidence/acceptance.md`, `scripts/release-evidence.mjs` — verify: `npm run verify -- acceptance-local`

## Dependencies and execution order

The default execution is strictly sequential in the order listed, with one writer per code worktree. No `[P]` marker appears. The only parallelism is the two Phase 2 writer lanes defined below (sequencing amendment A-005). Two dependency rules span the whole list: no package is added after T027, and the runner is frozen at the A-007 runner commit (earlier baselines: a71b3bc, then f31842b under A-006).

**Phase 1.** T001 → T026 → T001 hardening round (A-006) → T002 → T027 → T001 runner round (A-007) → T002 platform-inventory follow-up (A-007). Each runner round reuses T001's `files:` and verify command and moves the freeze baseline. The T002 follow-up reuses T002's.

**Phase 2: two disjoint writer lanes (amendment A-005).** Both lanes start only after Phase 1 (T001, T026, T002, T027) is accepted.

- **Lane A** (main code worktree `worktrees/001-corporate-signing-mvp/code`, feature branch `001-corporate-signing-mvp`): T003 → T028 → T029 → T030. That is storage, then the blob store, then the audit chain and retention, then outbox and restore.
- **Lane B** (second code worktree `worktrees/001-corporate-signing-mvp/code-lane-b`, temporary branch `001-corporate-signing-mvp-lane-b`, cut from the accepted Phase 1 head after T027): T004 → T033 → T005 → T031 → T032. That is fixtures and the dev launcher, then the parse service (which needs the T004 fixtures), then auth, then the server, then notifications.

**Lane rules.**
- **Disjoint by construction.** No `files:` path or directory prefix of a lane A task overlaps one of a lane B task. No Phase 2 dependency edge crosses lanes. Neither lane lists `package.json`, `package-lock.json` or a frozen runner file. A lane B task must not import `src/storage/` or `src/domain/canonical/`. The one exception (N3, A-010) is T031's post-merge SEC-07 tenant policy: `src/server/tenants.ts` may open the database and read the tenants row through the tenant-filtered store, with no writes and no migrations, and refuses an unmigrated schema. Its test support in `tests/server/` may build a migrated test database. Conversely, and a lane A task must not import `src/server/`, `src/api/`, `src/pdf/`, `src/notifications/` or `fixtures/`. Any task found to overlap would run sequentially instead.
- **One writer per worktree, per-task checks unchanged.** Each lane has exactly one writer. Each worktree fetches its own runtime with `sh scripts/fetch-node.sh` into its own gitignored `tools/`. Every task keeps the full orchestrator checks: the freeze diff against the current runner freeze commit (A-007), scope, the task's verify, `no-secrets`, the transcript model check, and regression of every Phase 1 mode plus the earlier modes of the same lane.
- **Integration.** The orchestrator cherry-picks each accepted lane B commit onto the feature branch, in lane order, with no squash and per-task commits preserved. A conflict stops integration and is reported; it is never resolved by editing task files. Lane A commits land directly on the feature branch.
- **After both lanes are integrated:** every Phase 2 mode (`storage`, `blobs`, `audit-chain`, `outbox-restore`, `dev`, `parser-limits`, `auth`, `server`, `notifications`) is re-run on the integrated head. Then the phase-end regression reviewer and the validation runner run. Finally the lane B branch is deleted and the `code-lane-b` worktree removed.
  - **Cleanup ruling (A-010).** The same applies to the fix-lane worktrees `code-fix-p1` and `code-fix-p2` and to the local branches `001-corporate-signing-mvp-fix-p1`, `-fix-p1b`, `-fix-p1c`, `-fix-p2` and `-t002-li11`. The orchestrator removes each worktree and deletes each branch only when its worktree is clean and `git cherry 001-corporate-signing-mvp <branch>` shows no `+` line, meaning every commit is integrated by fast-forward or patch-equivalent cherry-pick.
  - Any unintegrated commit stops that cleanup and is reported, never discarded.
  - All of these are local only; the remote holds just the feature branch, `main` and `docs`.
  - `code-us1` and its branch stay until T006 is integrated.
- **Later phases.** US1 and every later phase stay strictly sequential in the main code worktree, unless a later amendment says otherwise.

**Phase 2 close criteria (A-008).** Phase 2 is not closed, and the MVP is not reported ready, until every item below holds:

1. Every ledger row marked close-blocking is fixed in its owner task's `files:`, with the named test present and passing in that task's mode.
2. The material findings P02, P03, P08 and P09 are fixed **and** independently rechecked by a read-only reviewer. P03's independent re-check was first done in the phase-end review: proof-c, with RET-02, RET-03 and DB-26 present (receipts table 7; N2, A-010).
3. The Phase 2 regression reviewer's proofs are rerun on the pinned Node 24.22.2 (`tools/node/bin/node`) against the integrated head, and each must show its defect rejected.
   - proof-a covers P02, proof-c covers P03, and proof-d covers P08.
   - P09 (parent trusts child report, PL-17) is covered by the lane B p09-probe.
   - proof-e proves P05/[10], not P09, and is recorded as supplementary.
   - The PL-24 report-budget evidence never substitutes for P09.

   The outputs are recorded in the Phase 2 close receipts (N1, A-010).
4. Every Phase 2 mode passes on the integrated head, together with the Phase 1 modes and `no-secrets`.
5. The A-007 runner round (including N4 at ce36f6e and Codex [1]) is accepted and re-frozen.
6. The A-009 rows marked close-blocking are done. Two sets of probes are rerun on pinned Node 24.22.2 against the integrated head, and each must now show refusal or isolation:
   - the T030 reviewer's probes h1, h1c, h2 and the T30R-4 orphan scenario (T30R-1..T30R-4; OR-15..OR-24, OR-26). At the integrated head the original h1 is vacuous, h1c's target was never published, and h2 counts lock refusals as lost updates. The adapted copies, with their hashes and defect-present controls, are the receipts (table 6a; N4, A-010);
   - the lane B re-check probes, including the pathname-socket connect (LBR-1, PL-18).

   Their outputs are recorded in the Phase 2 close receipts.
7. The A-010 rows marked close-blocking are done, and these pending re-checks have reported PASS: the R5 fix of the no-secrets gate, bounded by the closure boundary (R5-1..R5-3; R4-1..R4-5 passed on e1be709); the lane B follow-up (LBR3-1, PL-24, passed on d037f99; LBR2-1..LBR2-3 passed on dcb8fce); the post-merge lane B follow-up (LBR4-1: PL-25, SEC-10); the T030 round 3 (deviation 2, T30D-1, T30D-3, T30D-4 and T30R2-1..T30R2-6; OR-14, OR-26, OR-28..OR-36); and the 10,001-revision probe with RET-08 ([16]). Passed so far: R5 at 666d14f (integrated by fast-forward from ce36f6e), T030 round 3 at f3de87a, and RET-08 at 306a84e. The lane A follow-up for T30R3-1 (DB-29) must also pass. Its re-check is bounded to DB-29, OR-37..OR-39, OR-41, OR-42, BL-12 and no regression of the accepted modes.

A mode PASS alone never waives a finding.

**Phase 2 close receipts (A-010).** The receipts live in the spec leg at `specs/001-corporate-signing-mvp/receipts/phase-2-close.md`. The Architect, as sole spec writer, writes that file from the evidence that the validation runner and the phase-end regression reviewer report. It is committed in the same commit as the Phase 1 and Phase 2 [X] marks, and only once every table below shows its pass condition.

Recording rules for every table:
- No host-absolute path appears anywhere in the receipts.
- A file is named by its repo-relative path, or by name plus sha256 when it lives outside both repositories, as proof scripts and logs do.
- A commit is named by its full 40-hex SHA.
- Any output is quoted verbatim, one decisive line, with secrets removed.
- A cell that cannot be filled is a failure, never a blank.

1. **Header** (`| Key | Value |`). Keys:
   - `code_head`: the integrated feature-branch commit on openSig-code;
   - `code_tree`: the tree object of that commit;
   - `tree_digest`: the runner's tree digest at `code_head` (plan.md § Runner rules);
   - `spec_head`: the spec commit whose ledger is checked;
   - `tasks_sha256`: sha256 of tasks.md at `spec_head`;
   - `runtime`: the `tools/node/bin/node --version` output and the binary's sha256;
   - `runner`: sha256 of `scripts/verify.mjs`, `scripts/verify-modes.mjs` and `tests/tooling/verification-runner.test.mjs`, each equal to its value at the freeze baseline ce36f6e;
   - `recorded_at`: UTC time;
   - `recorded_by`: the role and model of each recorder, with no personal data.
2. **Integration** (`| Step | Source commit | Integrated commit | cherry-pick -x trailer present |`). One row per commit integrated into `code_head`: lane A, lane B, the post-merge follow-ups, the fix lanes and T002 LI-11. Pass: every row resolves, and `code_head` descends from them all.
3. **C4 modes** (`| Mode | Owner | Final line (verbatim) | Exit | result.json sha256 |`). One row for each of the 13 modes owned by T001, T026, T002, T027, T003, T028, T029, T030, T004, T033, T005, T031 and T032, all run at `code_head`. Pass: every row is `PASS exit=0` with `fail=0 skip=0 todo=0`. CAPABILITY_ABSENT is environment-blocked and fails the table.
4. **C1 close-blocking rows** (`| Ledger row | Owner | Test ID | Mode | File:line at code_head |`). One row per test ID named in each close-blocking ledger row, counting only the named part for rows marked "yes (… part)".
   - Presence is the repo-relative file and line where a test whose name starts with `[ID]` sits inside that mode's test glob.
   - Passing follows from presence plus that mode's PASS in table 3. The runner runs every listed file and passes only with `fail=0 skip=0 todo=0`.
   - An amended, extended or restored ID needs its amended assertion present. The reviewer confirms that in table 6.
   - A test cell that is a proof or documentation points to table 5 or 6.
   - The table ends with a totals line: rows, test IDs and failures, which must be 0.
5. **C3 proofs** (`| Proof | Finding | Script sha256 | Exit | Decisive output (verbatim) | Log sha256 | Defect rejected |`). Rows: proof-a (P02), proof-c (P03), proof-d (P08), the p09-probe (P09, PL-17), and proof-e (P05/[10], supplementary), each rerun at `code_head` on pinned Node 24.22.2. Pass: `Defect rejected = yes` in every criterion-3 row (N1).
6. **C6 probes** (same columns, with `Targets` in place of `Finding` and `Refused or isolated` in place of `Defect rejected`).
   - Rows: h1 (T30R-2), h1c (T30R-3), h2 (T30R-1), and the T30R-4 orphan scenario.
   - Plus every probe named in the lane B re-check reports for LBR-1..LBR-6, LBR2, LBR3 and LBR4, the pathname-socket connect (LBR-1, PL-18) included.
   - The lane B rows also record the launcher used (bubblewrap, or `unshare`+`chroot`+`setpriv`) and the label `netns+fs-enforced (local)`. The gate `parser-network-sandbox` stays OPEN.
   - Pass: every row is `yes`.
7. **C2 and C7 re-checks** (`| Re-check | Scope (rows, test IDs) | Commit(s) reviewed | Verdict | Notes ruled in ledger rows |`).
   - Rows: the P02, P03, P08 and P09 independent re-checks (criterion 2), and every re-check named in criterion 7.
   - Pass: every verdict is ACCEPT or ACCEPT-WITH-NOTES, and every note has a ledger row or a ruling.
8. **C5 runner** (`| Item | Evidence |`). The A-007 round acceptance (reviewer verdict and commit), the `runner` hashes from the header, and `tooling` PASS from table 3.

**Who records what.**
- The **validation runner** records the header except `spec_head` and `tasks_sha256`, plus tables 2, 3 and 4. It runs every mode through `npm run verify -- <mode>` on the pinned runtime at `code_head` and does nothing else.
- The **phase-end regression reviewer** records tables 5, 6 and 7, and confirms the amended assertions behind table 4. It is read-only and reruns each proof and probe at `code_head`.

**[X] marks.** A Phase 1 or Phase 2 task gets [X] only when its mode row in table 3 passes and every close-blocking row it owns passes in table 4.

**Phase 2 closed (A-010).** Phase 2 is closed at code head `353e8c81e48634bdcea9cb0cff7d14ab37e50b1c` by `receipts/phase-2-close.md`. All 13 Phase 1 and Phase 2 tasks pass tables 3 and 4, so all are marked [X]. The MVP is not ready.

- the OR-35 notes T30R41-N1 (the fd-entry stat sub-case, with OR-43 before T025) and T30R41-N2 (the CLI move-path evidence limit, stated in T025's receipt); T30R4-1 itself is accepted at b631ebf (A-010, batch #6);

**Phase 3 checkpoint, the US1 independent test (A-010, request #39).** US2's commits do not start until it closes. Scratch-only preparation of US2 tasks may proceed meanwhile.
- **Who:** an independent agent that wrote none of the US1 code or tests (T006–T011, T034, T035 and their follow-ups) and did not accept them. A fresh agent is used: the driver asked for a separate acceptance, and the US1 recheck reviewer rechecks the SHELL-HASHCHANGE fix. Its model is checked in its transcript and recorded.
- **On what:** the corrected published code head after row SHELL-HASHCHANGE closes. It is named by its full 40-hex SHA, confirmed by `ls-remote`, and run in a clean detached worktree at that SHA. If any commit lands on the code branch before the checkpoint closes, the run starts again on the new head, because one checkpoint describes one head. When SHELL-HASHCHANGE closed, that head was 79f1863570c79530809ba2bf03c084a400c04081; the agent still confirms it with `ls-remote` (request #39).
- **What runs, serially, with the agent as the sole runner:**
  - `npm run verify -- us1` first, as the independent test itself;
  - then every mode the T011 acceptance ran (all 21, with `autograph`, `ui-build`, the US1 unit modes and no-secrets);
  - then `npm run typecheck`.
  - Every mode must PASS with zero fail, skip or todo. `us1` needs cases 6/6 with the chromium capability present; CAPABILITY_ABSENT is not a pass.
- **The evidence:**
  - the run's `.verify-out/us1/evidence.json` labels: `browser_engine` chromium, `browser_version` 149.0.7827.55, and `tree_digest`;
  - the tree digest recomputed independently from git objects at the head, equal to that label and to every mode's `result.json` `tree_digest` and `tree_digest_after`. A null `tree_digest_after` in any counted result is a finding, never a pass;
  - each of the six case IDs (SC004-01..SC004-04, US1-01, US1-02) as the leading `[ID]` of a passing test;
  - one decisive output line per mode, verbatim;
  - typecheck's exit status, and the pinned Node's version and binary sha256;
  - the run window and load, and the agent's confirmation that no process of its own remains.
- **Where it is recorded:** a spec receipt, `specs/001-corporate-signing-mvp/receipts/phase-3-checkpoint.md`. The Architect writes it from the agent's evidence, under the Phase 2 receipts' recording rules: no host paths; files by repo-relative path, or by name and sha256; full SHAs; verbatim decisive lines. Its header table follows table 1 of `phase-2-close.md`.
  - `docs/evidence/us1.md` stays T011's evidence for its own tree (0035817, digest 9ea3e8ee…3fbe) and is not updated for later trees: every later commit would make it stale again.
  - That doc is never cited as evidence for a later head. The receipt carries the corrected tree's evidence.
- **What "closed" means:** the receipt shows every item above, and a "Phase 3 checkpoint closed" paragraph here names the head and the receipt. That paragraph is what lets US2's commits start. There is no checkbox, because the checkpoint is not a task.
  - A failing case keeps the checkpoint open. The failure is recorded in the receipt as a failure and never relabelled. The owner fixes it in a follow-up, and a new full run on the new published head follows.
  - The hosted review gate (code#2) does not block the checkpoint, just as it did not block Phase 2's close.

**Phase 3 checkpoint closed (A-010, request #40).** The Phase 3 checkpoint, the US1 independent test, is closed at code head `79f1863570c79530809ba2bf03c084a400c04081` by `receipts/phase-3-checkpoint.md`. A fresh independent agent ran `us1` first, then the other 20 modes of the T011 acceptance, then typecheck. Every mode passed with zero fail, skip or todo (608 tests); `us1` passed cases 6/6 on chromium 149.0.7827.55; typecheck exited 0; and the independently recomputed tree digest equals every label. US2's commits may start. The hosted review gate (code#2) stays OPEN and does not block.

**US2** (needs T010; its commits also need the Phase 3 checkpoint to close, request #39, which closed at 79f1863, request #40).
- T012 comes first, written independently.
- The T012 follow-up slot (rows T012-EVDIGEST, T012-ANCHORS and T012-EDGES; request #41) runs after T013 is accepted and pushed and before T036 starts, and in any case before T014. It landed at e7ea3f1, so T014 is no longer blocked by it (request #43).
- T013 → T036 (needs T033) → T037.
- The T036 follow-up slot (rows SIZE-BOUND, T036-NUMRANGE and T036-FIXES; request #44) runs before T037's commit. Its writer makes one T033 commit for the indirect `/SigFlags` report, then the T036 commit (request #45). The T034 follow-up slot (EA-07; request #44) runs after it and before T038.
- The T036 follow-up slot gains a third commit, a T036 commit for row T036-RESIDUAL, before it lands (request #46). The T008 follow-up (row EVENT-CAP) and the T007 follow-up (`field_too_small`) run after the T036 follow-up and before T038, and the T008 follow-up also comes before T016's commit. The T034 follow-up runs after the T036 and T008 follow-ups (request #46).
- The T011 test-only slot of row US1-MASK, then the T031 slot of row SEC16-PROBE, run right after the T036 follow-up slot lands, before T037's commit and before the T008, T007 and T034 follow-ups (request #47; order by request #48).
- T014 needs T012 and T037 output.
- T039 → T016 (needs T014 and T029; A-010, S4).
- T038 needs T016, T014 and T028–T030.
- T015 needs T038.
- The T031 US2 composition slot (row ARTIFACT-ROUTES; request #46) runs after T016 and T038 and before T017.
- T017 needs all of the above plus T009.

**US3** (needs US2).
- T018 needs T034.
- T019 needs T030.
- T020 needs T016.
- T021.
- T040 needs T018 and T031's socket listener.
- T022 needs T040, T020, T021 and T009.
- T041 needs T040.

**Release.** T023 needs T026, T002 and T027. T024 needs T030. T025 runs last.

**Story independence.** US1 is independently demonstrable human approval but must not claim sealed completion. The releasable signing MVP requires US1 and US2. Feature acceptance also requires US3 and the cross-cutting gates.

**Harnesses and fixtures.** T006 and T012 are harnesses and never count as runtime acceptance. Handler and engine acceptance lives in T034 and T013/T036/T037/T014/T038.

**Capability-gated modes.**
- `autograph`, `us1`, `us2-local` and `us3` need chromium.
- `integrity-contract`, `cms`, `verification` and `us2-local` need openssl.
- `hermes-runtime`, `us3` and `us3-real-model` need hermes-runtime.
- `us3-real-model` also needs claude-sdk-transport.
- The external gates in `acceptance` are not task verifies.

| PL11-ESC-LIM named limits of the PL11-ESC design (requests #14, #16 and #17). (1) An orphan, or a child re-captured while its launcher's stop is pending past the cap, is killed after an identity re-check, leaving a microseconds-wide window. A launcher that is not yet stopped can reap such a child between capture and kill. Reusing that pid needs about a full pid wrap inside the window, and Node exposes no `pidfd_send_signal` (past-cap path named by request #21). (2) A child forked less than one poll tick before an UNEXPECTED launcher death cannot be captured, and this slot does not close that. Such a child is bounded only by (a) the inherited RLIMIT_CPU, which bounds CPU time only; (b) the parser's own limits, or natural completion; and (c) separately evidenced launcher-death behaviour: bwrap's `--die-with-parent` once the child has armed it (O1 observed SIGKILL pending), and nothing under util-linux. Idle wall time and RSS are NOT bounded by these. No fresh wall enforcement reaches a child outside the poller's view. Settlement, separately, still waits for stdio to close (precision amendment to request #20). (3) If the service dies inside the stop window, a util-linux `unshare` can be left stopped with one zombie; bwrap is covered by `--die-with-parent`, and row ULX-SVC-DEATH's supervisor control covers both. (4) The stop cap is a timer: about 100 ms plus timer lateness (116 ms observed at load about 73 under the no-signal mutant). (5) In a normal bwrap teardown, the launcher can exit before its namespace init (O1, lifecycle 3 of 3 at load 96; consistent with upstream bwrap v0.9.0 `do_init` and `monitor_child`, cited for reference only). The service then ends the init through its captured identity although its death signal is already pending, and counts it. (6) A launcher that is in uninterruptible sleep when the stop is sent delays the kill, and therefore settlement, until it stops or exits. The delay is counted, not logged (request #21), and a late child is still ended (request #20) | INFO (recorded limits) | T033 (+T024) | No change in this slot for (1)–(4); T024 documents (2) and (3), including the operator's cleanup of a stopped `unshare`. `orphans_ended` counts the captured children the service actually ended, benign init cleanup included. WIRE-US1 or T024 decides how it is logged | PL-26 (amended) | Recorded with the T033 product slot; T024 | no |
| ULX-SVC-DEATH under util-linux, when the service process alone is SIGKILLed, `unshare` and the worker survive; the worker runs until it ends, and `unshare` then exits. Under bwrap the whole sandbox was gone within 30 ms (`--die-with-parent`). With the service's whole process group killed, both launchers' sandboxes were gone within 30 ms. Observed identically on the PL11-ESC tree and on pristine 789a3f7, so it is pre-existing and not a PL11-ESC regression | LOW (fallback launcher only; bounded by RLIMIT_CPU and the parser's own limits) | T033 (+T024) | No change in the PL11-ESC slot. T024 documents the control: the service runs under a supervisor that stops it by killing its whole process group or cgroup | — | T024 | no |

| T036-RESIDUAL six more ways an upload-accepted document could strand after approval, found by the T036 follow-up writer, the T034 EA-07 preparation and the slot recheck (Q6). (1) `bounds.ts` drops a key named `__proto__`, so a page with `/__proto__ 0.0000001` escapes the predicate and the composer refuses it once the page gets a widget (a LOW defect). (2) `xref-size`: a huge trailer `/Size`. (3) `page-box`: a page coordinate above 1,000,000, or a page side under one centipoint. **Rulings that need no row of their own:**
- **`TenantStore.list()`.** It fails loudly with `StorageLimitError` past its cap; T003 adds a keyset iterator (DB-24, DB-25). That is the root cause of P05, [9], [10], [13] and [14], and those callers iterate anyway.
- **No-secrets closure boundary (A-010).** The no-secrets gate is a heuristic, defence-in-depth control. It never replaces reviewing changes for secrets, and a PASS never certifies that a tree holds no secret. After the R5 round:
  - the gate's independent re-check covers only the ledgered NS cases (NS-01..NS-18), a no-loss superset check (every sample the previously accepted scanner reports is still reported), and a regression check against that accepted commit;
  - a new finding is close-blocking only if it is a regression or a realistic HIGH fail-open;
  - any other new finding (pre-existing and heuristic) is ledgered as non-blocking and documented as a known limitation in T024's threat model, and it does not reopen T026.
- **T007 decisions (A-010).**
  - **Migration 0011 is ratified.** A digest alone cannot return the original result that the contract's Idempotency-Key promises. Its rules are in data-model.md § IdempotencyRecord: at most 256 KiB, no capability values, a 24 h lifetime with bounded purge, not evidence. Owner T007; test ST-13, plus T034's EA-02.
  - **Service sends.** The contract lets `service` call `sendEnvelope`, and T005 classifies service principals as role `service` only. The spec's rule that no service-initiated send happens without a human confirming the recipient preview (spec.md clarification; SC002-24) applies to every service send. A SendConfirmation is therefore required when the envelope is service-created **or** the sender is a service. Owner T007; test ST-14, plus T034's SC002-24.
  - **Contract text.** The contract's `send_confirmation.required` field ("True when created_by.kind is service") stays accurate for the creator rule. Its description gains the service-sender rule at the next contract bump, with no schema change.
  - **Recorded without change:**
    - a reissue that copies the document ends at version 2;
    - T010, not T007, inserts the SigningJob when the envelope enters `finalizing`;
    - `advanceOnApproval` refuses an expired envelope inside the caller's transaction, and T010 commits the expiry in its own transaction or leaves it to the sweeper;
    - `uploadDocument` relies on T034 having stored the blob through T028.
- **T008 deviations (A-010, 3b61502).** None of these rulings changes 3b61502 before push; migration 0013 stands.
  - **D1, production wiring.** T031 owns `src/server/` and `tests/server/`. One T031 US1 composition slot, after T010 and before T035, wires all US1 handlers and composes their dependencies (row WIRE-US1). T008, T034 and T010 each export a handler map and test through it; none adds `src/server/` to its `files:`.
    - SEC-04 keeps guarding uniform errors and the registry. Every registry route either has a handler from the composed maps or stays null and answers the uniform 501 `Error` with a correlation ID, and no handler exists outside the registry.
    - SEC-05 replaces each 501 it used to mean "not dispatched" with the contract's own boundary outcome for that request (401, 403 or 404 `Error`). A route still without a handler keeps 501. No assertion is dropped.
    - The setting is `OPENSIG_CHALLENGE_KEY_FILE`, documented in quickstart.md § Configuration and by T024.
    - T008 may be marked [X] once accepted. The wiring row blocks T009 and T011, not T008.
  - **D2.** CH-01..CH-03 are ratified as the writer defined them; the T008 line now states them.
  - **D3.** SC002-14's setup is accepted. A storage-level move to `declined` is a legal transition, and the graph trigger enforces that. The subject under test, the real reissue together with resend and void, uses real code. T011 exercises the real decline path end to end.
  - **D4.** Ratified: invitation expiry is immutable after issue, and invitation and challenge rows are never deleted.
    - No MVP path deletes ceremony rows. Retention disposes blobs, and tenant retirement only records a ledger entry.
    - A future retention task that must erase ceremony data must remove an envelope's whole ceremony set in one audited transaction, and must amend the no-delete triggers by an appended migration.
  - **D5.** Accepted as an interim. SC002-16's positive case asserts only that a valid token passes the CSRF guard, never the 501 itself, so it stays valid after T010. T010's AP-03 completes it.
  - **D6.** Ratified for the single-instance pilot. In-memory limits only throttle request rates; every security counter stays durable (challenge attempts, cumulative wrong codes, supersession). A multi-instance deployment needs a shared limiter, which T024 documents.
  - **D7.** Assigned. The spec forbids links, tokens and codes in audit events, which presumes ceremony events exist, and the audit receipt is the time-ordered evidence of mailbox-control assurance behind each approval. T008's follow-up writes those events (CH-06, row CEREMONY-AUDIT).
  - **Main-worktree order** (A-010, batch #3, requests #4, #5 and #9, and batch #6). Pushed so far: the corrected T031 SEC-11 (58f88da and the round-4 R3-3 commit 5361d4b), the T033 PL25-ORC slot (7a02449), the T030 T30R4-1 slot (b631ebf), the T008 follow-up (23cd368, and round 2 at 0eba6ec) and the T033 PL11-WALL slot (789a3f7). Also pushed since (on the code branch): the T007 slot (384df35), the T008 follow-up (6a5a8e2) and the T033 product slot (PL11-ESC, with KILL-RSS and KILL-CPU; 6d63cbc + a4e6996). Pushed since request #22: the T006 test-data follow-up (195ff8a), T034 (83d9ac0), T010 (53d6c9f), the T010 follow-up (8f6ccc7), the T034 follow-up (cdae49f), the T031 US1 composition (WIRE-US1, 96b12c5), the post-composition follow-up (90b6f85, 219ca9d, 631e1b8 and 917b6a6), T035 (f710e1f), T009 (f609c89 and d10ca65), T011 (0035817) and the T009 SHELL-HASHCHANGE follow-up (79f1863). US1's tasks are complete (requests #23 to #39). The Phase 3 checkpoint closed at 79f1863 (request #40). In US2: T012 (88e10c7), T013 (b41ae17), the T012 follow-up (e7ea3f1), T036 (909172b) and T039 (c17d220); the T036 follow-up slot (with its one T033 commit) and the T034 follow-up slot are queued (requests #41 to #45). Queued after the driver's scope for this run: the T030 OR-43 touch before T025, the T024 documentation items, US2, US3, Release and the final reconciliation (request #38).
- **Request #48 (A-010): the SEC16-PROBE (T031) and US1-MASK (T011) preparation rulings, the interim conditions during the two slots, and their order.** No checkbox changes, and no `files:` or `verify:` change. Both preparations are static, and nothing was run. The raw failures stay preserved, SEC-16's product-level assertion is kept, the Phase 3 receipt is not reopened, no timeout is altered, and no synthetic load is used.
  - **Part A, SEC16-PROBE** (the T031 line; prep report `evidence/sec16probe/SEC16PROBE-prep-report.md`, 6db48373…).
    - (A1) **The T031 line and row SEC16-PROBE, with this bullet and #47's, are the authority for the amended SEC-16.** T031's SEC cases have always been defined in its line, so plan.md gets no SEC block.
    - (A2) **The own-listener rule: adopted.** A LISTEN socket on the former port whose inode is among the test process's own file descriptors fails the test, whatever in the process holds it. The product runs in that process, so such a socket is an untracked product listener, or a test helper on the product's port; either is a fault. Without the rule, the new check would pass a case the old one failed. The snapshot reads `/proc/net/tcp` and `/proc/net/tcp6`, filtered to the port, and only the test's own descriptors: it inspects nothing it does not own, as #47 (3) requires. A connection that reaches another process's listener stays a recorded diagnostic.
    - (A3) **Yes: a request or refusal log line from the product during the drain counts as the probe reaching the product.** A refusal is the product answering, which "no new work can start" excludes.
    - (A4) **`.verify-out/server/sec16-probe.json` is acceptable**, under C1.
    - (A5) **The relay socket and its 401 premise are within "amended without weakening".** #47 asks that every product listener be closed while the drain waits, and the relay is one when configured. Its premises assert real product behaviour, so a failure there is a real failure of what they assert. Each premise has its own assertion message, so it can never carry SEC-16's interim signature.
  - **Part B, US1-MASK** (the T011 line; prep report `evidence/us1mask/US1MASK-prep-report.md`, 915ae0bf…).
    - (B1) **All ten places are fixed in this slot**, the four more included. They are the same defect class inside T011's `files:`, and leaving them would keep SC004-02 and SC004-04 masked. Phase B adds one injected-failure run and one reverted mutant for each of `mobile.test.mjs` and `link.test.mjs`.
    - (B2) **Both the message line and the record file**: `.verify-out/us1/first-error-<case>.json`, under C1. The frozen evidence labels are unchanged.
    - (B3) **Yes: the record covers the part of the test where pages are open.** Before that there is no page state to record, and an error surfaces unmasked anyway.
    - (B4) **Appending the record line to the error's own message is "amended without weakening"**, if the original message stays first and unchanged, every check is unchanged, and the line holds no invitation link, token, cookie value or host path.
    - (B5) **U1 and U2 are committed** under `tests/acceptance/us1/`, so the masking fix stays guarded in every `us1` run. They never lead with a required case ID: an extra ID is allowed, and a required one would let a probe stand in for a browser case. **Mutants may run under the frozen runner** in a scratch git copy that holds `tools/` and `node_modules/`. Never the main worktree, one at a time, each mutant's diff verified, timeouts unaltered. Their results are kill evidence, not acceptance.
    - (B6) **Yes, the cleanup is hardened.** Each close runs in its own guard. A cleanup error joins the record and never replaces the first error, and the original error is rethrown. This also settles row T011-NOTES (3), the unguarded `after` hooks. T011-NOTES (1) and (2) wait for the next touch after this slot, because (2) adds a bound and this slot alters no timeout.
    - (B7) **The one changed assert line is acceptable:** it is the same check with the same message, reading the status from the settled response.
  - **Part C, shared.**
    - (C1) **Diagnostic records live in the runner's per-mode output directory**, `$OPENSIG_VERIFY_OUT` (`.verify-out/<mode>/`): ignored output, never committed, outside the tree digest. A record is a diagnostic, never evidence of a PASS, and the frozen runner's checks do not read it. It holds no secret, token, invitation link, cookie value or key, and no host-absolute path: stack frames are repo-relative.
    - (C2) **The reading is confirmed, and the order is swapped: US1-MASK first, then SEC16-PROBE**, both right after the T036 follow-up slot lands and before T037's commit and the T008, T007 and T034 follow-ups.
      - The US1-MASK commit changes only `tests/acceptance/us1/`, which nothing in `src/server/` imports. So its `server` run stays under SEC16-PROBE's interim condition, and a US1-01 failure there is REAL and unmasked.

    - Observed: the raw failure, "the listeners are closed while the drain waits: no new work can start", `false !== true`, after a fresh TCP connection to 127.0.0.1 on the product's former port succeeded during the drain. In 5 of 5 instrumented diagnosis runs, the product showed `listening: false` and a null address 0.1–0.2 ms after `close()`, before `draining`; no connection event reached any server in the test process after the close; and the probe was refused. The bench's container shares the host's network (the driver's read-only inspection).
    - Static: commit 2 changes only `src/cryptograph/compose/` and `tests/compose/`, nothing outside them imports the composer, and `src/server/` and the test are unchanged since 128633e. There is one product listener, on an OS-chosen port; the relay socket is unset; the SMTP relay holds its own port; and the runner runs files one after another.
    - Not observed: which listener accepted the raw connection. Hypotheses H3 (a self-connect), H4 (a foreign host listener) and H5 (anything else) stay open, and none is a cause here.
    - **The classification rests on a static fact, not on the non-reproduction.** After the product closes, its former port belongs to no process, and on a bench that shares the host's network, any listener may hold it. So a successful connection there does not show that the product accepted work, and the probe's oracle can fail without any product fault. Whether that happened in the raw run is not observed. Nothing shows the product accepting work after its close.

    - Observed: the raw failure, `page.waitForResponse: Target page, context or browser has been closed`, recorded as an unhandled rejection at 15.6 s, before the 30 s page timeout, so no Playwright step timed out. In runs B–D, every watched request reached the product with its expected status, every response wait resolved, no crash or page error occurred, and the browser disconnected only in `after()`.
    - Static: under the pinned `node:test`, the rejection was charged while the test body ran, so the page closed then. Under the pinned Playwright, that wording means a close without a reason, either the test's own `context.close()` or a browser disconnect, and not a crash. At six places the test starts a wait and awaits its action separately, so an action that fails first is hidden behind the wait's rejection. Commit 2 cannot reach the `us1` paths, which are unchanged since 128633e.
    - Not observed: the first error, its step, and the page state. U1 (the test's own cleanup after a fast action failure) and a browser disconnect stay open, and neither is a cause here.
    - **The masking is a test defect regardless of the cause:** it removes the evidence that would classify the failure. No product defect is shown, and none is excluded beyond commit 2, which cannot reach these paths.

The runs never overlap, and timeouts are unaltered. Both outputs, the command, the commit, the windows and both load-average pairs are recorded verbatim.
