The diagnosis runs are evidence, not the rerun, because their launcher was not the runner's command. So exactly one serial rerun of each command at 5db851e is still needed: `server` first, then `us1`, never overlapping.     - **(B1) Compatibility. The mathematical bound: yes.** A 10 MiB base plus `UPDATE_BOUND` reaches 101,051,200 bytes of final PDF, over T028's 64 MiB, so a package can exceed it too. **The measured sizes: yes, in practice.** Honest bases composed to 782,902–11,267,676 bytes, far under every bound. An upload-accepted adversarial base of 4,082,540 bytes composed to 90,546,288 bytes, 23,437,424 over 64 MiB. The mathematical bound is therefore reachable, and the chain must hold to a bound that covers it.
    - **One consistent bound: a sealed PDF is at most 48 MiB (50,331,648 bytes), `MAX_SEALED_PDF_BYTES`, enforced at upload.** T036 exports it, with a predicate that gives each document's worst-case composition from its parse report, taking every page as a possible widget page.
      - T034 refuses at upload a document whose worst case exceeds it. T036 refuses above it as an invariant.
      - T037 and T014 accept documents up to it (the 16 MiB guesses go).
      - T016 keeps the package within T028's 64 MiB, so the other four members and the ZIP overhead get at most 16 MiB.
      - T028's default, the server's store and backup and restore (`--max-blob-bytes`, `blob_max_bytes`) keep 64 MiB. **No configuration changes.**
      - 48 MiB is far above every measured honest composition (at most 11,267,676 bytes) and leaves 16 MiB of the store limit for the package's other members. The adversarial base's 90,546,288 bytes would be refused at upload.
    - **(B2) Where it fails today:** at 909172b, T036 composes such a document. The T037 draft would refuse it above 16 MiB, non-retryably, so the job would fail at once. Past that, the T014 draft would refuse it above 16 MiB. Past that, T038's store would raise `BlobTooLargeError` above 64 MiB, a failure that was unclassified. Each happens after approval, and each would strand the envelope. **Under this ruling, such a document is refused at upload and never reaches finalization.** A store-time `BlobTooLargeError` is non-retryable and marks a broken invariant (the T038 line).
    - **(B3) The remedy: a scoped variant of option (a)**, with the refusal moved to upload, where request #43's principle puts refusals of documents. Option (c), raising T028's default to 128 MiB, would also keep every upload sealable, but it changes configuration that the driver asked to keep, so it is not ruled. Option (b) cannot work alone: a page that shares its `/Annots` array with others still needs its own copy.
    - **(B4) Timing:** the T036 follow-up slot, which adds the bound, the predicate and the scoped number check, comes before T037's commit. The T034 follow-up slot, which applies the predicate at upload (EA-07), comes after it and before T038. T037, T014, T016, T038 and T024 take the bound in their own tasks. Lines: T036, T034, T037, T014, T016, T038 and T024; plan.md CP-01, CP-08, WK-04 and VB-06; data-model.md DocumentRevision.

Writing plain decimals was not chosen, because it breaks the update bound (a 6-character exponent form can need more than 300 digits). The slot is the T036 follow-up, then T034's.   - **Part B, the T036 preparation (the composer).** The draft is the proposal; each ruling is in the T036 line unless noted.
    - (Q1) **The CP catalog is ratified as proposed** and defined in plan.md § Case catalogs (CP): CP-01 the update, CP-02 the form, CP-03 rotation, CP-04 appearances, CP-05 the `/Sig` dictionary, CP-06 determinism, and CP-07..CP-09 as the T036 line states.
    - (Q2) **One `/FT /Sig` field, with one widget kid per manifest field in manifest order: ratified.**
    - (Q3) **`/M` is the time of the `envelope.seal_started` event that anchors the manifest being sealed**, passed as `sealTime` by T038 (the T038 line). The composition evidence and the seal time then share one anchor, and the composer reads no clock. If a retry writes a new anchor, its `/M` follows that anchor. **`/Reason` carries the label**, worded for people: "Organization seal. The signing time is unsigned server time, not a trusted timestamp."
    - (Q4) **The visible date shows the date only.** A label inside a small field would shrink the date to nothing under the metric-free bound. The date widget's annotation `/Contents`, its accessible text, reads "Date from unsigned server time (UTC)", and the manifest and verification report carry the label as before.
    - (Q5) **Licensing: no AFM data ships or enters the repository** (row AFM-WIDTHS). The owner's rule (plan.md § Owner decision: platform license scope) holds shipped assets, fonts included, to MIT or Apache with no waiver. Adobe's AFM notice is neither, and a width table taken from the AFM files is derived from them. The conservative option is the metric-free bound of one em per character, so text is small but always fits. **Any other width source, such as independently derived widths with recorded provenance, is a policy decision that only the driver can make**; this ruling does not make it.
    - (Q6) **The `/ID` policy is ratified:** the base's first identifier is kept, and the second is a SHA-256 prefix of the revision's content (a base without `/ID` gets the content identifier twice).
    - (Q7) **Existing unsigned signature fields and `/NeedAppearances` are left alone.** Refusing at composition would strand an envelope that recipients have already approved, over a document accepted at upload. Any refusal of such documents belongs at upload (T033), not here. A later change to the document after the seal is a new revision, which coverage reports.
    - (Q8) **Each refusal carries a `retryable` flag**, true only for 429 `rate_limited` and the parse service's 500s. A non-retryable failure (400, 413, 422, and the composer's own 500s: `report-invalid`, `document-digest-mismatch`, `certificate-invalid`) moves the job to `failed` at once. A retryable failure follows the bounded retry. Repeating a deterministic refusal cannot succeed, and an operator's `retryFinalization` still applies after a fix. This is in data-model.md § Transitions and races, item 3, and the T038 line.
    - (Q9) **No separate output cap.** The input is at most 10 MiB, and the field, stroke, point and report-node budgets bound the update. The header states the worst-case update size, and CP-01 composes the largest request the budgets allow and stays under it.
    - (Q10) **The capability gap is recorded, not registered.** The frozen registry stays as it is. The `compose` mode, like every mode whose tests run the real parse service, FAILs on a host without bubblewrap or `unshare`; it is never CAPABILITY_ABSENT. quickstart.md § Runtime and commands states this bench prerequisite.
    - (Q11) **The clash is designed out:** the seal field's partial name is `opensig-seal-` followed by the first 16 hex digits of the document digest. A base field cannot carry a name derived from the digest of the document that contains it. Refusing dangling form references is ratified. No verifier finds the seal by name.
    - (Q12) **`/F 132` (Print and Locked) and `/SigFlags 3` (SignaturesExist and AppendOnly, combined with the base's flags) are confirmed.**
    - **In-memory test keys meet the key rules.** The file rules govern key material that touches disk. A key held only in memory, never written, exported or printed, exposes less (data-model.md § Authentication and secrets).
    - **Read-only imports are acceptable.** Reading other modules' exported constants and interfaces is not a `files:` change, and it is preferred to restating them, because a restated constant can drift.
  - **Part C, the T039 preparation (the ZIP writer).** Each ruling is in the T039 line unless noted.
    - (Q1) **`writeZip(entries, limits?)` is the interface**, with `{ name, data }` entries; T016 calls it (the T016 line).
    - (Q2) **T039 owns only the format ceilings** (the ZIP64 escape values minus one) as its defaults. Product bounds are T016's to pass, and a caller may lower the limits, never raise them.
    - (Q3) **Entries are sorted by name bytes.** package-index.json's `files` order is T016's own and need not match the ZIP's order.
    - (Q4) **Names are flat ASCII:** letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-`, 1 to 255 characters, not starting with `.`, with case-insensitive duplicates refused. The only caller writes five fixed names, so the narrow rule removes the file-and-directory clash, normalization, bidi and traversal classes by construction instead of by extra checks.
    - (Q5) **Unix host, version 2.0, regular-file mode 0644, internal attributes 0, and the UTF-8 flag always set**, as the T039 line already requires. Extracted files are then never executable.
    - (Q6) **An empty entry list is refused.**
    - (Q7) **ZIP-01..ZIP-06 are registered as extra case IDs** in plan.md § Case catalogs (ZIP); the frozen registry requires none. The independent reader may live in a helper under `tests/zip/`, kept independent of the writer.

    - **The writer's reproduction on 0035817** (Chromium r1228) failed behaviourally: no invitation step, the token in the address bar, one history entry holding it, and focus on the body.
    - **The fix:**
      - A `hashchange` listener leaves a fragment without `i` alone. It scrubs any fragment with `i`, duplicate or malformed included, synchronously with `replaceState`, and restarts the flow only for a valid invitation.
      - The restart aborts the previous flow's requests and moves a generation counter on. It clears all step state and live regions, shows the invitation step with focus on its heading, and sends nothing. Every continuation checks its generation. Ending the session is not aborted.
    - **AG-08** (4 tests):
      - (a) a follow from `/sign`; (b) a second follow on A's review; (c) A's held challenge, released after B's follow, leaves B untouched; (d) the skip link is untouched; (d2) duplicate and malformed `i`; (e) no token leak.
      - (a) and (b) carry a same-document proof: a `window` marker survives, one `hashchange` fires, and no document is requested.
      - The writer's mutants SH1–SH8 were all killed. SH8, a `location.reload()` instead of an in-place restart, is killed only by the same-document proof.

    - (Q1) **The proposed OC map is ratified, with two additions forced by Q5:** a seal on a cross-reference-stream base (OC-01), and a bad cross-reference-stream entry (OC-08). The definitions are in plan.md § Case catalogs (OC). OC-09 is an extra case beyond the registry's OC-01..OC-08.
    - (Q2) **The OC-09 splice is admitted, with conditions.**
      - The attribute's DER is the oracle's own, never `src/`. The new signedAttrs SET is signed by OpenSSL (`dgst -sha256 -sign`, RSA PKCS#1 v1.5), and kept only if `openssl cms -verify` passes.
      - A negative control shows the spliced CMS with the old signature fails. A failed build stops the run; it never skips the case.
      - OpenSSL stays the cryptographic ground truth: a SET that is not proper DER re-encodes differently and fails verification, so a splice mistake fails closed.
    - (Q3) **The verdict mapping is ratified, with one tightening:** acceptance needs `artifact_match` true, not merely "not false". Every corpus case supplies the recorded digest.
      - Integrity is `valid` or `invalid` on the corpus.
      - Trust is `indeterminate` unless integrity is `valid`, and is judged against only the case's anchors.
      - Coverage is structural, and acceptance ignores trust.
      - T014 agrees on integrity, trust, coverage, the artifact digest and its match, and the approval-evidence digest and check, plus the count. It uses T012's public exports only, in its tests.
    - (Q4) **A signingTime in a foreign CMS is accepted and reported.** It never changes a verdict, and T014 reports it as a profile check (`signing-time-present`); product-sealed output must show it absent. The product's own profile forbids signingTime (T013), and the oracle's OpenSSL-made CMS carries it by default.
    - (Q5) **The oracle must read cross-reference streams.**
      - T004's base set already includes one (`agreement-xref-stream.pdf`, with object streams), and real uploads commonly use them. The composer appends onto whatever base is uploaded, so the base's stream stays in the `/Prev` chain whatever the composer emits.
      - The T036 composer is not yet written. It appends a section of the same kind as the base's last one, which is the conservative practice.
      - So T014's agreement on product-sealed output, and T017's per-flow oracle result, would otherwise read "unsupported" on a supported product path.
    - (Part E follow-up) **Hybrid files are in scope.** T033's parse service accepts them (its parser reads a classic trailer's `/XRefStm`), so an uploaded hybrid reaches the composer. The oracle reads them; the corpus adds a seal on a hybrid base (OC-01) and a bad `/XRefStm` offset (OC-08). T036 appends a table after a hybrid base, whose last section is a table. So T014's agreement and T017's oracle results cover hybrid bases too.
  - **Part F, the T013 CMS-profile rulings** (from the fresh T013 writer's scratch draft; needed before T013's Phase B):
    - (Q1) **The catalog** is plan.md § Case catalogs (CMS): KEY-01, KEY-02, DER-01..DER-05 and CMS-S01..CMS-S06, with the draft's IDs. The registry is frozen, so CMS-01 is an extra case, like OC-09.
    - (Q2) **signatureAlgorithm is sha256WithRSAEncryption with NULL parameters** in the product profile. T014 also accepts rsaEncryption, which OpenSSL-made CMS in the oracle corpus may carry.
    - (Q3) **The sid is issuerAndSerialNumber, SignerInfo version 1**, copied from the certificate. T014 also accepts a subjectKeyIdentifier sid that matches the embedded certificate.
    - (Q4) **ESSCertIDv2 holds the certHash only.** hashAlgorithm stays at its SHA-256 default and is omitted, and issuerSerial is omitted: the certificate travels in `certificates`, and the hash binds it. T014 accepts either form, because OpenSSL-made CMS may carry issuerSerial.
    - (Q5) **Request #30's owner-only rule**, not exactly 0600: a regular file owned by the server user with no group or other permission bit (0600 or 0400), opened without following a final symbolic link and without blocking. Exact 0600 would refuse 0400, which is stricter.
    - (Q6) **No pipe.** The descriptor is open on the key file itself. T038's runner opens the key file and hands the descriptor to T037's worker, and T013's adapter checks it there: regular file, owner, mode and size by `fstat`. For a path it first refuses a relative path or a symbolic link. A pipe would hide the custody facts the checks need.
    - (Q7) **The env/argv refusal is accepted** (KEY-02): loading refuses while PEM private-key text is visible in env or argv, and a refusal names a code, never a path or key bytes. The settings are `OPENSIG_SEAL_KEY_FILE` and `OPENSIG_SEAL_CERT_FILE`. T038 reads them, the production composition validates them at startup in a T031 slot ruled when T038 approaches, and T024 and quickstart.md § Configuration document them.
    - (Q8) **Bounds.** `/Contents` holds exactly 2 × `signedDataLength(certificate)` hex digits, with no padding (T036), and T037 refuses a CMS of any other length. The certificate is at most 16 KiB of DER. The chain is the signer certificate only; intermediates belong to the open `trusted-certificate-key-custody` gate. The key file is at most 16 KiB. The public exponent is exactly 65537.
    - (Interface) **Ratified:** `buildDetachedSignedData({ contentDigest, approvalEvidenceDigest, certificateDer, signer })` over two 32-byte digests returns DER. `contentDigestOf` gives the SHA-256 over the ByteRange bytes in order, the key stays inside the module behind a `SealSigner`, and equal inputs give identical bytes. `signedDataLength` is exported for T036's placeholder.

- **Request #32 (A-010): two WIRE-US1 timing doubts, and a correction to request #31.** WIRE-US1's Phase B is running. Point 1 needs a code change in the running commit; point 2 does not.
  - (1) **REQ-TIMEOUT: ruled otherwise, with no copied constant and no T033 row. The composition settles the parse plan at startup. This is a CODE CHANGE in the running WIRE-US1 commit.**
    - Why the probe was in the bound: T033's parse service awaits its plan (`planFor`, memoized per process) before it takes a body-read permit and reads the body. So the first upload in a process waits for the sandbox probe, up to 10 s per launcher kind.
    - The change: after the composition is built and before either listener exists, startup awaits `parseServicePlan()`. T033 exports it as the production plan, and it shares the uploads' memo, so no upload then waits for the probe.
    - The `listening` line logs the plan's mode and launcher, as a warning when the plan is not sandboxed. The server still starts, and uploads keep T033's own answers.
    - `PRE_BODY_WAIT_MS` is then the storage busy timeout alone (the recipient session guard's transaction), read from `DEFAULT_BUSY_TIMEOUT_MS`. So `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` is 20 s + 640 s + 5 s = 665 s with today's constants. The restated 10 s is removed, and nothing of T033's is copied (the LBR4-1 rule).
    - Tests: SEC-10's relation uses the new terms; SEC-12 shows that the `listening` line carries the settled plan; a new mutant that skips the startup settle must fail.
    - The cost: when a probe hangs, startup takes up to 10 s longer per launcher kind, once per process. T024 documents it.
    - Rejected: keeping the restatement with a row for T033 to export the constant. It copies a T033 value, against LBR4-1, and needs two more commits in two owners' files.
  - (2) **A pipelined request's wait: accepted as a documented limit, with no change in this slot** (row PIPE-WAIT, INFO, T024).
    - The app dispatches a connection's requests one at a time. So a pipelined request waits for those ahead of it, and Node's request timeout runs while its own body is not yet read.
    - It is bounded in practice. SEC-11 closes the connection after a complete upload body, so nothing queues behind an upload. SEC-08 closes after a decided `Connection: close`. Each request ahead is therefore a non-upload request bounded by its own deadlines. A request without a body is complete once its headers are parsed, so the timeout no longer applies to it.
    - Only a pipelined request that has a body, waits behind slow requests, and is itself sent near the minimum rate can pass the timeout; the unused part of the header allowance gives some margin. Its own client chose that, it reaches no other client or tenant, and the answer is 408 after the pending response (SEC-13 (a)). Browsers do not pipeline.
    - T024 documents it: a client should not pipeline an upload.
  - (3) **Correction:** the review request for cdae49f was made at 02:26:09Z, GitHub's comment `created_at`. Request #31 gave 02:26:12Z, the orchestrator's local time after the command returned. The request #31 bullet is corrected.

    - **The overdue upload's pre-body write lock: accepted under UPLOAD-OVERDUE, with no new row.**
      - The expiry is the write that UPLOAD-OVERDUE requires before any body read, and it takes the write lock as every write does (data-model.md § Persistence).
      - While another process holds the write lock, it holds the event loop for up to the busy timeout (5 s). The T024 line already documents that cost for every write (request #30).
      - It masks no transport answer. The 415 and If-Match answers come before it, and no body is read after it, because the answer is 409 and the connection closes.
      - It happens at most once per envelope. Once the expiry commits, the lock-free pre-check sees `expired` and answers 409 without the lock. An expiry that fails busy is tried again by the next request, as any write would be.
      - Not observed at runtime, by design.

The recipients world (`tests/recipients/support.mjs`) gains T034's operator handlers but has no authenticator, so its operator routes stay 401 before any handler runs.

    - (2) **The dev launcher: its owner, T004, in the post-composition follow-up slot** (row DEV-COMPOSE).
      - Not a Phase B block. DV-03 runs a stand-in entrypoint in a temporary layout, and runs the real script only with `--check`, so the composed server cannot turn `dev` red, and every commit stays green.
      - Until the slot, `node scripts/dev.mjs` starts the composed server only if the developer supplies the key file and a migrated database. The launcher passes `OPENSIG_CHALLENGE_KEY_FILE` through, because its secret-name filter does not match that name.
      - The server never creates a key or a database. Development keys are generated at runtime outside the tree (data-model.md § Keys and secrets).
    - (3) **SEC-07's one 501 becomes 404: approved, under D1's SEC-05 rule.**
      - The readEnvelope assertion now meets the real handler. Its 404 `not_found` must be shown to come from that handler (its log line names the operation), so it still proves that the allowed role passed authorization, as the 501 did.
      - The tenant_admin retry keeps 501, because `retryFinalization` (T038) has no handler yet. No assertion is dropped.
  - **Part B: semantics.**
    - (4) **Key-file mode: "no group or other access", the current loader's rule.**
      - The property that matters is that no other user can read or replace the key. Exact 0600 would refuse 0400, which is stricter. The owner-execute and special bits change nothing for a data file read through a descriptor.
      - The T031 line and quickstart.md § Configuration now say "no group or other permission bit (for example 0600 or 0400)", with the loader's 32 to 4096 byte bound.
      - Phase B adds a 0400 acceptance case and a group-readable refusal case to SEC-12 where they are missing.
    - (5) **SEC-13 (c): the uniform 400 `Error`.**
      - Once a handler was entered, the request belongs to the application, so T031's uniform-error rule applies (a contract `Error` body with a correlation ID). The line's "Node's 400" meant the status and the promptness, not Node's canned response.
      - Still required: the 400 comes right after the pending response, then the connection closes, with no 10 s idle wait, and the permit is released at once. The T031 line and row SEC11-Q are amended.
    - (6) **SEC-13 (b): the documented T024 limitation.** `httpAllowHalfOpen` is undocumented, so it is the "more internals" the line names, and it would need its own SEC-14-style guard. The optional patch is not applied. Row SEC11-D5 names T024 for (b), and the T024 line carries it.
    - (7) **The blob root `<OPENSIG_DATA_DIR>/blobs`, created 0700 if absent: confirmed, with no new setting.** It sits beside the default database path. quickstart.md § Configuration names it, and T024 documents it as the directory that backup's `--blobs` names.
    - (8) **Two connections with operator auth on: acceptable.**
      - The policy connection only reads, with single statements and no transaction. Every transaction on the composition's connection is synchronous. So the two never hold overlapping transactions within the process.
      - Both are opened through `openDatabase` with the same busy timeout, and both are closed after the drain. Sharing one connection is not required.
      - The database is required with or without operator auth, because the recipient routes need it. The composed server opens an existing, migrated database and never creates or migrates one, as SEC-07 already does: confirmed, and quickstart.md says so.
  - **Part C: product paths without an owner.** Each row states its exposure.
    - (9) **The expiry sweeper: T031, in this slot** (row SWEEP-SCHED). The composition is the only place that holds both the database and the ceremony's listener, and `main.ts` is T031's.
    - (10) **Parse-count logging: assigned to this slot** (row PARSE-COUNTS). T024 is documentation and cannot log, so leaving the counts to it would leave them unread.
    - (11) **`requestTimeout`: SEC-10's minimum-rate rule is the intended body bound** (row REQ-TIMEOUT).
      - Node's request timeout is only the outer backstop, so it must not be shorter than the longest request SEC-10 admits.
      - The writer confirms whether anything makes a request wait before its body read starts; if something does, its bound is added.
    - (12) **Shutdown: a fix is needed, in this slot** (row SHUTDOWN-INFLIGHT).
      - SEC-16's drain covers only the ceremony's post-response work, not request handlers. This slot introduces the database close, so it owns the order.
      - Handlers are awaited before the ceremony drain, because a handler can still schedule ceremony work.
    - (13) **The FIFO race: T008 owns the fix, in the post-composition slot** (row KEY-FIFO).
    - (14) **The drain bound: documented by T024, with no code change.** Every step has a deadline except a spool write, which rests on T024's existing single-host local-filesystem assumption. Row T008FU-D9 and the T024 line state the bound.
    - (15) **The writer's additions inside T031's files: accepted, on three conditions.**
      - The `createAppServer` options parameter defaults to today's behaviour, so SEC-01..SEC-11 pass unchanged apart from the amendments ruled here.
      - `App.routes` exposes the same frozen registry, read-only.
      - The `fail()` deferral has a test that fails without it (a named case and a mutant). Otherwise it is dropped.
  - **Part D, recorded (16).** The T034 follow-up carries the driver's arrival-time read finding (row T034R-LOCK).
    - The amended commit is cdae49f (parent 8f6ccc7). It replaced the unpushed e2119ea.
    - `hasLiveRecord` and the upload pre-check now read with no transaction.
    - It adds two `[EA-02]` held-lock cases (415 in 3 ms; a streamed over-limit 413 in 48 ms) and mutants J1 and J2.
    - Acceptance is running, and the row closes with the follow-up.
  - **(17) T007's reads under the write lock: a T007 follow-up is needed** (row READ-LOCK-ENV). The same class exists in T008 (row READ-LOCK-CER, found while ruling).
    - **Exposure.** The routes are GETs, so no transport answer is masked.
      - Within the process nothing waits, because every write runs synchronously on the composition's one connection.
      - The stall needs another process holding the write lock (an operator tool, or a second instance against D6's single-instance rule). Each such read then holds the event loop, and with it every listener and tenant, for up to 5 s, and then fails busy.
    - **A read with no transaction is enough; a DEFERRED snapshot is not required.**
      - The read's statements cannot interleave with this process's own writes, because those run synchronously.
      - Every envelope mutation re-checks its state and version under the write lock (and `confirmSend` re-checks the preview digests), so a view torn by another process cannot cause a wrong change.
      - A snapshot would also need a new T003 helper, outside T007's files.
      - The rule is now in data-model.md § Persistence: a request path that only reads opens no transaction.
    - **Slot: the post-composition follow-up, right after WIRE-US1 and before T035.**
      - Nothing is deployed before T024, so WIRE-US1 exposing the routes first opens no real exposure, and WIRE-US1 does not wait.
      - The same slot carries T004's DEV-COMPOSE and T008's KEY-FIFO. One writer makes one commit per owner, each inside its owner's `files:` and green on its own.
    - **Accepted with no change:** T010's sweeper reads its due list inside its transaction. Every envelope it finds is then expired under the write lock anyway, so a lock-free list would save a stall only when nothing is due.
  - **Rows:**
    - Opened: SWEEP-SCHED, PARSE-COUNTS, REQ-TIMEOUT, SHUTDOWN-INFLIGHT, KEY-FIFO, DEV-COMPOSE, READ-LOCK-ENV, READ-LOCK-CER and T034R-LOCK.
    - Amended: WIRE-US1, SEC11-D5, SEC11-Q and T008FU-D9.
    - No checkbox changes.

- **Request #26 (A-010): T010 at 53d6c9f, seven points. None blocks T010's push.**

It composes the exported `expiryAnnouncer` into EnvelopeApi's expiry path, with a test (EA-06) asserting exactly one event. It does not block T010's push. It lands before the T031 US1 composition, and SEC-12 then sees it composed.
  - (4) **The decline reason code: keep it.** Request #24 (3) barred secret or personal values: tokens, challenge codes, session values and field values. The contract's enumerated decline reason is categorical metadata and the only record of the reason. Only the enum value may be stored, never free text.
  - (5) **Body validation inside the write transaction: accepted.** It is synchronous, CPU-only work over a body capped at 256 KiB (15,000 points), with no I/O or await inside the transaction. The AP-01 worst case's validation time is logged as a diagnostic of lock hold time, with no pass bound.
  - (6) **413 and 415 before the session guard: confirmed for T010.** These are transport answers from the server, consistent with request #25.

- **Request #25 (A-010): two T034 follow-up points on key precedence.**
  - **(1) Transport and precondition checks before the key: accepted.** Request #23's "key before body validation" covers checks on the body's content, schema or semantics. A 409 needs the body compared against the stored request digest, which in turn needs a complete, well-framed body of the declared media type. 413 (too large), 415 (media type) and 408 (body timeout) are framing answers that come before any comparison is possible. Upload's If-Match check (400 or 428) concerns a header precondition, not the payload. All of these keep their own answers under a reused key, with no mutation and no record written.
  - **(2) Client abort under a reused key: ruled otherwise.** An aborted body was never compared, so it cannot be a conflict. It is logged as the abort, under T031's existing abort mapping, the same as an abort under a new key, and nothing is written. That keeps 409 meaning only "a completed comparison differed". The writer applies this in the follow-up before Phase B.
- **Request #24 (A-010): T010 Phase A, nine items.** "Change" below means T010's code or tests must change before its runs.
  - (1) **SC002-02: plan amendment, no change.** The session-bound approval and decline routes carry no envelope id and declare no 404. A session of envelope A that names envelope B therefore gets 409, identical to a binding mismatch on A itself, with no write in either tenant. plan.md's SC002-02 and the coverage list are amended. The contract does not change. The writer's 409 test stands.
  - (2) **New row ROWID-APJ, LOW (defence in depth): no change to T010's runs.**
    - Owner: T010, whose files include `src/storage/schema/`, which writes `approvals` and inserts the reservation.
    - Fix: migration 0018, applying the full trigger fix of request #13 to both tables. Delete is refused, and legitimate job updates stay allowed. AP-13 is extended to both tables in all three foreign-key modes. If a production path must delete or re-key these rows, stop and report.
    - Slot: a T010 follow-up after T010's push. It does not block T010's push, but it blocks the T031 US1 composition.
  - (3) **Audit events: accepted, conditionally.**
    - Required: an approval event and a decline event, each in the same transaction as its change.
    - The expiry and reservation events are accepted, provided none duplicates an event T007 already writes for that transition.
    - Metadata holds refs and digests only, never field values, tokens, codes or session values.
    - Change only if an event breaks these conditions.
  - (4) **`max_attempts = 5`: accepted, no change.** data-model.md § Transitions item 3 bounds the retries without naming a value, and migration 0004 allows 1–100 (corrected by request #26; it was first given as 1–10). Five is the MVP default, now written into data-model.md's SigningJob row. Test fixtures may use other values.
  - (5) **Decline: CHANGE.** A decline revokes every other non-final recipient (`pending` or `invited` becomes `revoked`), mirroring void. A recipient who can never be invited is revoked, not left pending. data-model.md § Recipient status is amended, and the decline test asserts it.
  - (6) **Decline event: CHANGE.** The contract's WebhookEvent types include `envelope.declined` and `envelope.expired`, and an OutboxEvent is inserted in the same transaction as the change it announces. Decline emits `envelope.declined`. Sweeper expiry emits `envelope.expired`, unless T007's expiry path already emits it. Each fires exactly once, and nothing is emitted on a refusal.
  - (7) **Handler wiring: CHANGE, option (i).** T010 leaves T008's `RECIPIENT_HANDLERS` unchanged, because that map lies outside T010's `files:`, and exports its own handler map. AP-03 runs SC002-16's positive case end to end in T010's own test world, with an `ApprovalApi` composed. WIRE-US1 composes both maps, and SEC-12 proves that the real handlers serve. No dependency-absent 500 is reachable in T008's world.
  - (8) **Validation order: CHANGE.** Authentication comes first, so an unauthenticated caller learns nothing about validation. The order is: session (401), then the exact-retry receipt (a changed retry, malformed or not, gets 409, consistent with requests #16 and #23), then the body (400 or 422), then the remaining checks. The session is still re-read inside the transaction.
  - (9) **Purge boundary: change only if it differs.** The purge must delete exactly what T007's replay-expiry predicate already treats as expired, using that same predicate rather than a restatement. A purge can then never delete a record that replay would still honour. If T007 treats a record of exactly 24 h as expired, the writer's inclusive `<=` stands; otherwise it must become strict. AP-02 tests the boundary instant.

- **Request #21 (A-010): two wording points from the #20 follow-up.**
  - **(1) PL11-ESC-LIM (1) is widened explicitly, and only, to name the past-cap re-capture.** While the stop is pending, the launcher is not stopped and can reap a child between capture and kill. That leaves the same identity check-then-kill window: the re-check runs immediately before `kill()`, and a reuse needs about a full pid wrap.
  - **(2) Stops past the cap: option (a), the #15 (c) precedent.** The service exposes a process-wide, read-only count of stops that went past the cap, beside `parseCapacity()` and the orphans count, with no logger in `src/pdf/`. The late-fork test asserts that it increments. The lifecycle record (`stop_past_cap`, `stop_ms`) stays the test evidence. Logging belongs to WIRE-US1 or T024. LIM (6) now says "counted" instead of "logged".
- **Request #20 (A-010), with the driver's steer: a multithreaded init, and the stop-cap fallback (N-a).**
  - **(1) Multithreaded init: option (a), a product and test change in this slot.**
    - The settle condition becomes: each captured child is reaped, or is a zombie whose `Threads:` count is 1, read through the same captured `/proc` handle. This holds trivially for bwrap's single-threaded init, and it closes the formal gap for util-linux's multithreaded Node init. No new reliance on behaviour is added; the check is only stricter.
    - Check (d) inspects captured identities and their task entries instead of command lines, because a zombie leader's command line is empty.
    - Bound: the wait polls on the existing tick, and the test asserts it stays below one default wall period.
    - The request #18 basis is corrected in place.
  - **(2) N-a, compared explicitly with PL11-ESC's coverage.** The slot claims that a decided kill ends the service's own whole sandbox, including a process that starts after the decision. The fallback as built violates that claim, so this is a residual PRODUCT path, not a test gap.
    - Exposure: the stop does not land within the cap, which in practice means the launcher is in uninterruptible sleep, and a fork lands after the last capture or is in flight. The fallback then kills only the earlier snapshot and the launcher.
    - Cover per launcher: under bwrap, `--die-with-parent`, but only once the child has armed it (the run-14 window). Under util-linux, nothing.
    - Bounds on such a child, for the pre-fix exposure: it is bounded only by (a) the inherited RLIMIT_CPU, which bounds CPU time only; (b) the parser's own limits, or natural completion; and (c) separately evidenced launcher-death behaviour: bwrap's `--die-with-parent` once the child has armed it (O1 observed SIGKILL pending), and nothing under util-linux. Idle wall time and RSS are NOT bounded by these. No fresh wall enforcement reaches a child outside the poller's view. Settlement, separately, still waits for stdio to close; that is a statement about settlement, not a bound on the child (precision amendment to request #20).
  - **Ruling for N-a: CORRECTION in this slot. The hard claim stands, and its wording is unchanged.**
    - The launcher is SIGKILLed only once it is observed stopped, or has already exited.
    - While its stop is pending past the cap, every tick re-captures its children through ownership and SIGKILLs each through identity. A live launcher keeps its children capturable, because reparenting happens only at the parent's exit, and a stopped launcher cannot fork.
    - A launcher stuck in uninterruptible sleep delays settlement until it stops or exits. SIGKILL could not act on it sooner either. The delay is recorded as PL11-ESC-LIM (6) and counted, per request #21.
    - Deterministic seam evidence is required: a test-only hold, reachable only via `createParseServiceForTests`, keeps the launcher from stopping within the cap while it forks a late child. The test asserts the late child is ended through its identity and the launcher is killed only after it is observed stopped. A stale-snapshot mutant must fail. No host load.
    - The remaining named limits are PL11-ESC-LIM (1), the identity check-then-kill window, and (2), a launcher that exits on its own.
  - **Process:** the PL11-ESC writer applies (1) and (2) as one follow-up commit on top of 6d63cbc. The orchestrator's acceptance and a0de559's delta recheck run at the new head, and the push follows. a0de559's thread-count structure run supplies observed `Threads:` values.

It is inside T006's `files:`, and EC-ENV-22 is the only `path-parameter` case, so it touches nothing else. The row now names the file. The patch keeps the case count at 72, and `npm run verify -- envelope-contract` must pass at its commit.
  - **(2) Expiry mid-upload: accepted.** A request's key is resolved once, at the start, and the record's 24 h lifetime is judged at that moment. A record that expires, or is purged, while the body is still being read stays in force for that request. That is the safe direction: it never causes a second mutation.

- **Request #18 (A-010): "terminated", not "reaped", before settlement; option (a).** This is a small batch, because the wording blocks the PL11-ESC push.
  - The normal-run property reads: every captured child terminated (a zombie or reaped) by settlement, and reaped within one default wall period of the channel closing; nothing of the owned tree or namespace runnable after settlement. That is D2 as accepted at 15bd5ac, and 6d63cbc satisfies it.
  - Why a zombie init is enough: when a PID namespace's init terminates, the kernel kills every other process in that namespace (pid_namespaces(7)). The init becomes a zombie only once they have exited, which is kernel behaviour and inferred here, so a terminated init means its namespace is empty. **Corrected by request #20:** this holds only once the init's LAST thread has exited. While a live thread remains, the kernel hands the reaper role to that thread without emptying the namespace (`find_child_reaper` and `find_alive_thread`, in the running WSL2 6.18.33.2 kernel source, cited for reference only). So a multithreaded init, such as util-linux's Node worker, can show a zombie leader while threads still live. Settlement therefore requires a captured child to be reaped, or a zombie with `Threads:` = 1.
  - R10 observed both inits already reaped at settlement: bwrap 14.8 ms and util-linux 16.2 ms after the channel closed.
  - Strict reaped-before-settlement (option b) is not required.
  - If a0de559's static recheck finds that a terminated init does not guarantee an empty namespace, this comes back for a ruling.

- **Request #14 (A-010): the PL11-ESC design's residual windows; the reorder.**
  - **Scope: met, for proof in Phase B.** On a decided kill, the stopped launcher cannot fork. Its captured children, including the PID-namespace init, are SIGKILLed through captured identity. The init's death ends every process the namespace started, including one that started after the decision, and settlement waits for every captured init to end (D2). The wall budget still runs from spawn; the stop adds at most 100 ms, logged. Spawn options are byte-identical to 789a3f7, with no `detached` and no group signal, which removes the service-death regression of the group design. The design touches only the service's own tree, with no global scan, no host load and nothing as root. Q3 stays inference until the structure case runs on the actual launchers. Phase B must include the driver's service-death check for both launchers.
  - **(1) Orphan check-then-kill window: accepted and recorded (PL11-ESC-LIM).** The identity re-check (pid, start time, `/proc` handle) runs immediately before `kill()`. Reuse inside microseconds would need about a full pid wrap, and the worker has no child-process grant. A race-free form needs `pidfd_send_signal`, which Node does not expose, and no new package may be added. Revisit if the pinned runtime gains it.
  - **(2) A child forked less than one poll tick before an UNEXPECTED launcher death: accepted and recorded.** It needs an external death of the launcher, not a decided kill, inside about 10 ms. Settlement still waits for stdio to close, so no outcome settles while that child holds the output pipe. The child itself is bounded only as PL11-ESC-LIM (2) states, as restated by the request #20 precision amendment: RLIMIT_CPU for CPU time only, the parser's limits or natural completion, and bwrap's armed death signal. Idle wall time and RSS are unbounded. The decided-kill path is race-free.
  - **(3) Service death inside the stop window, util-linux only: accepted and recorded.** A stopped `unshare` and one zombie can remain, and the work inside is bounded as in (2). bwrap, the preferred launcher, is covered. T024 documents the operator's cleanup. Closing it is not required in this slot.
  - **(4) The orphan outcome as 422 `resource-limit`/`cpu`: accepted.** It is the existing mapping of a SIGKILLed launcher, the same one the kernel backstop already uses. The cleanup contract is met before settling, so this is not the forbidden 500-while-running case. The service log records `orphans_ended`.
  - **(5) fd 3 under each launcher, and Q3:** proven in Phase B by the structure case. If a launcher cannot be proven, stop and report.
  - **Reorder, recorded:** T007 slot (384df35, pending acceptance) → T008 follow-up (0016) → T033 product slot (PL11-ESC, reapplied onto the pushed head with full acceptance) → T034 → T010 (from 0017) → T031 US1 composition → T035 → T009 → T011.
- **Request #13 (A-010): the hidden-rowid route past the no-replace triggers.**
  - **Classification:** a defence-in-depth bypass, LOW, the same class as T008-F3. It was observed in two probe runs at 789a3f7. It defeats REPLACE-ENV and ST-16 (T007; row ROWID-ENV) and the accepted T008-F3 and CH-08 (T008; row ROWID-CER). Nothing in `src/` passes a rowid; the store reads `rowid` only for paging.
  - **The fix:** the full trigger fix. It is minimal and schema-compatible: no table rebuild, rowid paging unchanged. `WITHOUT ROWID` is not needed, an insert-only check is not enough, and `recursive_triggers` would guard only the app's own connections. On each table:
    - BEFORE INSERT refuses a key that exists, or a rowid that equals an existing row's;
    - AFTER INSERT refuses a stored rowid of 0 or less;
    - BEFORE UPDATE refuses any change of the rowid or of the key. The key form of the UPDATE route is closed by the same trigger, and the tests must show it.
  - **Ordinary semantics, ruled explicitly:**
    - An insert that names no rowid stays valid.
    - A duplicate key surfaces as a `trigger` constraint on all four tables, and the app still reports `storage_constraint`. No production code may branch on the constraint kind.
    - `NEW.rowid` for an omitted rowid is undefined in SQLite, and is observed as -1. Because no stored row may have a rowid of 0 or less, -1, 0 or NULL can never match a row. Any other change would refuse inserts, which fails closed, and the ordinary-insert test makes it fail loudly on a runtime bump.
    - Rowids of 0 or less are refused, which removes the -1 lockout.
    - `rowid`, `_rowid_` and `oid` are one column, and the tests cover all three spellings.
    - Every UPDATE that changes a rowid or a key is refused.
  - **Delete triggers:** yes, in the same slots, on `envelopes` (0015) and `recipient_sessions` (0016). Without them, a plain delete and re-insert reaches the same reset, and the rowid fix on those two tables would be cosmetic. This is consistent with D4's no-delete stance for ceremony data. A future erasure task amends the triggers by an appended migration. At 789a3f7 the only store delete in `src/` is on `idempotency_records`. If any production path or another task's test deletes these rows, stop and report.
  - **Ownership, numbering and marks:**
    - The T007 slot carries 0015 (envelopes: I1, REPLACE-ENV, ROWID-ENV, I2).
    - A T008 follow-up slot, after the T007 slot, carries 0016 (invitations, challenges, recipient sessions), with CH-08 extended. T008 keeps its [X], as T031 and T033 did for their follow-ups.
    - Migration numbers follow push order. T010 and any later migration take the next free number when they are pushed: 0017 onward once both slots land.
  - **Order:** T033 product slot (PL11-ESC) → T007 slot (0015) → T008 follow-up (0016) → T034 → T010 → T031 US1 composition → T035 → T009 → T011. The T007 slot may go before the T033 product slot if it is ready first, since the files are disjoint.

The outlier stays an evidence limit unless it recurs after the fix.

- **Request #11 (A-010): the PL11-WALL slot accepted; KILL-COV split by path.**
  - **PL11-WALL is closed at 789a3f7.** The full acceptance passed with 468 tests and 0 fail, skip or todo. The short check was ACCEPT-WITH-NOTES. In it, the reviewer's own 21 s delayed-kill mutant was caught by the blocking case (21335.5 ms against 20000), while the real-parser case passed silently at 1204.4 ms. Line 65 measured 336.7 ms on unmodified code. The slot is pushed, and the interim condition ENDED with the orchestrator's acceptance record. From here, every `parser-limits` FAIL is REAL. The hosted review gate stays OPEN: a quota reply is not a review.
  - **KILL-COV is split into KILL-RSS and KILL-CPU; the V8 heap case is excluded.**
    - Heap: `heapMiB` relies on V8's `--max-old-space-size`. The child can die by V8 OOM with no parent signal, and the service then reports `memory` from its stderr. A no-signal mutant surviving that case is therefore not a defect in the heap-limit proof.
    - CPU (row KILL-CPU): the evidence is slot proof a-alt plus `service.ts:725`, which reports the decided reason whatever ended the child. If the parent's kill is missed, the kernel RLIMIT_CPU backstop (`ulimit -t`, ceil(cpuMs/1000) + 1 s) bounds the extra CPU to about one second. That backstop also ends a CPU-burning test worker and reports `cpu`, so the CPU case proves that the CPU limit ends the child, without telling the two mechanisms apart.
    - RSS (row KILL-RSS): the evidence is the same. This corrects 6217608's rationale. Neither RLIMIT_CPU nor the V8 heap cap bounds memory outside the V8 heap, and the launch sets no OS memory limit. A missed RSS kill is bounded only by the parser's own limits and the child's natural end. The RSS case does tell the failures apart: a missed trigger gives `wall-time`, and a missed signal hangs into the guard. RSS has the larger exposure, so its case goes first.
    - Placement, re-confirmed: both cases ride with any T033 product slot that PL11-SLOW causes; otherwise they go in a T033 test-only touch before T025, KILL-RSS first. T024's threat model states the RSS bound.

The 3989.3 ms real-parser call remains an observation with an unmeasured phase, not proof of a missed kill; the 315.4 ms blocking case is distinct. The CPU-first variant runs the PL-11 CPU case before the wall case, as the test itself does, and the slow run happened in that order. It is warranted only if the wall-only runs show no run over 1 s. Then up to 20 serial runs, with the same instrumentation and rules, report before T034 is pushed. Otherwise, it stays a recorded evidence limit.

- **PL25-ORC (A-010, request #4 and its addendum).**
  - **Evidence, kept apart.**
    - Observed: the natural failure, run 1 at the 58f88da acceptance (04:15–04:17Z), preserved verbatim: test 1 `'timeout' !== 200`, then test 2 `2 !== 0`.
    - Not observed: the original slow phase. The reviewer did not reproduce the natural failure; under natural load (20.1) each parse took 332–405 ms. The cause of the 3000 ms overrun is therefore inferred, not established. The sandboxed child is the dominant phase in the observed passing trace, and a candidate cause: one passing trace does not show that no other phase could exceed 3 s under scheduler stalls (driver wording correction, request #5).
    - Reproduced deterministically: the cascade. A 4000 ms delay injected in a scratch copy only gave test 1 `'timeout' !== 200`, then test 2 `2 !== 0`, and everything drained to 0 slots and 0 permits.
  - **Classification:** a test oracle and isolation defect; no product defect is shown. The cascade into test 2 is proved by reproduction. The test-1 overrun is classified on inference only: a wall-clock pass bound turned an unobserved stall into a FAIL, and the reproduction shows no slot or permit leak. The T033 slot retires the inference: once it lands, a stall long enough to fire PL-25's hang guard is REAL.
  - **Interim acceptance until the slot lands.** A `parser-limits` FAIL counts as PL25-ORC only if all of these hold:
    - the failing assertions are exactly the PL-25 honest-upload bound (`'timeout' !== 200`), optionally followed by the cascaded client-abort slot check (`2 !== 0`);
    - every other `parser-limits` assertion passed;
    - the commit changes no file under `src/pdf/`, `src/server/` or `tests/pdf-parse/`.

    Such a commit is accepted on exactly one serial PASS rerun of `npm run verify -- parser-limits` at the same commit on the pinned runtime. Both outputs, and the load average at each run, are recorded verbatim, and the record names the first FAIL a natural PL25-ORC occurrence whose overrun cause is inferred, not reproduced. A failing rerun, or any other signature, is REAL.
  - **58f88da.** It changes only `tests/server/pipelining.test.mjs`, so it qualifies, but it is not fully accepted until the condition is met. The 04:20Z serial run meets the condition only if it was exactly that command at 58f88da on the pinned runtime, with a PASS final line (37/37, `fail=0 skip=0 todo=0`) recorded verbatim. Otherwise, run one serial rerun now.
  - **Writer:** the T033 owner, lane B writer a416ce57afe222305, is recommended; the orchestrator decides.
- **T30R4-1 (b) (A-010, request #5).**
  - **Evidence, kept apart.**
    - Observed: at the 7a02449 acceptance (log written 09:02:20Z; load at the end 31.27 33.98 27.04), `outbox-restore` FAILED 40/41 and every other mode passed. The one failure is OR-35 with the refusal `process(es) 3941948 of this user cannot be inspected, so it cannot be shown that none holds the damaged lock; the lock was not moved`. The product failed closed, as OR-41 requires.
    - Code reading at 7a02449 (no run): the message lacks the CLI's `backup: refused ledger_invalid:` prefix, so a library call inside OR-35 threw it. Two such calls give the product the pids that the test's own two host scans saw as inspectable: the success path after a CLI refusal, and the 60 s rule. A same-user process can turn uninspectable after those scans, for example while it exits.
    - Inferred, not reproduced: the cause. Pid 3941948, a same-user host process, was transient or uninspectable when the product scanned. The only check made was that `/proc/3941948` no longer existed; nothing else was inspected and nothing was killed.
    - Evidence, not a verdict: one serial run at 7a02449 (09:05:41Z–09:07:30Z) PASSED 41/41.
  - **Classification:** inside T30R4-1, extended as (b). It has the same owner, test and fix family: OR-35's oracle models the host process table differently from what the product sees, and the product fails closed both times. It is not a product defect, and it is no reason to change lock safety, to inspect or kill unrelated processes, or to weaken OR-41.
  - **Interim acceptance until the T030 slot is accepted.** An `outbox-restore` FAIL counts as T30R4-1 (b) only if all of these hold:
    - exactly one assertion failed: OR-35, with the uninspectable-peer refusal above (any pid list);
    - every other `outbox-restore` assertion passed, with `skip=0 todo=0` and every case present;
    - the commit changes nothing in T030's `files:` (`src/storage/outbox/`, `src/storage/backup/`, `scripts/backup.mjs`, `tests/outbox-restore/`).

    Such a commit is accepted on exactly one serial PASS rerun of the same command at the same commit on the pinned runtime. Both outputs, the command, the commit and both load-average pairs are recorded verbatim, and the record names the first FAIL a natural T30R4-1 (b) occurrence whose cause is inferred, not reproduced. A failing rerun, any other signature, any OR-41 failure, or any run in which a lock was moved while a holder lived or a pid was uninspectable is REAL. The condition never applies to a receipt run.
  - **7a02449.** It meets the condition. Run 1 has exactly that signature, and the commit changes only `tests/pdf-parse/slow-body.test.mjs`. The 09:05:41Z run is the one serial PASS of `npm run --silent verify -- outbox-restore` at 7a02449 (41/41, `fail=0 skip=0 todo=0`, cases 4/4). Its log holds only the verify line, so the acceptance record adds verbatim the command, the commit, the window, both load-average pairs and exit 0. No further rerun is made.

- **R3-3 (A-010, request #4 addendum).**
  - **Finding.** `exchange()` in `tests/server/pipelining.test.mjs` writes the second packet on a 3 s dispatch fallback without counting a guard hit. A handler that is never reached fails later assertions, but a handler later than 3 s passes silently, without the "later packet" ordering its test names. The round-3 probe delayed A's handler by 3500 ms in the test that sends a malformed request while the upload handler is held: B went out early on the fallback and 9/9 passed. The outcome was right; the named ordering was not checked.
  - **Ruling: fix now (ratified).** The file's own header promises that every test asserts no barrier opened on its guard instead of its condition, and the fallback is such a barrier. This is the same class as R2-4b, which 58f88da fixed for `quiesce()`. A fired fallback counts as a guard hit and fails the test that awaits the exchange, with a message naming the dispatch fallback. The fix is test-only and inside T031's `tests/server/`; `src/server/` is unchanged, there is no spec change, and the test ID stays SEC-11.
  - **SEC-11 push.** It includes the round-4 commit on top of 58f88da, and every commit is green on its own (as in row ST11-FX). The round-4 commit is accepted when all of these hold:
    - its diff touches only `tests/server/`;
    - `npm run verify -- server` PASSes at it with `skip=0`;
    - the round-3 late-handler probe, re-run on a scratch copy, now fails and names the fallback, while the unmodified suite passes;
    - `parser-limits` at it PASSes, or meets the PL25-ORC interim condition above.

- **T008 review F1–F5 (A-010; ACCEPT-WITH-NOTES for 3b61502).**
  - F1: "generic 202" includes timing. The design is respond-then-process, not constant-time padding. The proof is deterministic (CH-07), and it lands in the T008 follow-up slot, before WIRE-US1.

- **T007 re-check notes (A-010; ACCEPT-WITH-NOTES for 68e0042).**
  - N1 is IDEMP-B1: migration 0012 adds the size trigger.
  - N2 is IDEMP-B3: T010's sweeper runs the purge (AP-02). Until then, an expired record is deleted only when its key is used again.
  - N3 is accepted: a stored `authorization_reference` or recipient email is no new exposure, under the same-principal replay, single use and the 24 h lifetime. T034's EA-04 proves that no capability value is stored once capabilities exist.
  - N4 is a migration rule, guarded by ST-15.
- **PL24-ORC classification (A-010).** FIFO admission does not order completions, so completion order is no invariant. Until the oracle fix lands, a `parser-limits` FAIL counts as the known PL24-ORC defect only if the sole failing assertion is a completion-order one (`the slot holders finish first`, or the completion-order FIFO inference) and every earlier PL-24 assertion passed. Such a failure is recorded verbatim. A commit that touches neither `src/pdf/` nor `tests/pdf-parse/` is accepted on a PASS rerun of `parser-limits`. Any other failure is a real failure. The failure observed on the tree that became f6adc3c is classified PL24-ORC.
- **ST-11 fixture walks (A-010, row ST11-FX).** This is option A; option B is rejected because it contradicts P04.
  - Legal walks: `completed` goes `draft → prepared → sent → finalizing → completed`, entering `finalizing` with `approvals_complete = true`. `sent` goes `draft → prepared → sent`. `declined` goes `draft → prepared → sent → declined`. `voided` and `expired` may go straight from `draft`.
  - Each step is a storage-level compare-and-set that moves the version by +1. It adds no audit event, outbox event or other row.
  - Intent is preserved. Each test keeps its terminal state, its event counts, chain heads, checkpoints and disposal sets, and every assertion; nothing is deleted, loosened or skipped.
  - Only literal versions derived from the walk may change, and each change is justified in the commit message. For example, `reserved_version` becomes the walk's `finalizing` version, which is 4 when the walk starts at version 1.
  - A test whose meaning needs a direct jump is reported as a block, never rewritten.
  - Order: one main-worktree writer, each commit with explicit pathspecs inside its owner's `files:`, and every commit green with full regression of the Phase 1 and Phase 2 modes. No intermediate commit may be red.
    1. The T030 test-only commit: OR-21's walk, plus the `envelopeVersion` inputs of OR-01 and `liveSystem` read from the envelope's current stored version. It passes with the old fixture.
    2. The T029 test-only commit: the `terminalEnvelope` walk.
    3. The T007 commit, with its verify `envelopes`.
  - Reading a test input from the database, such as the envelope's current version, is acceptable. It derives the same quantity and is not a loosened assertion.
    - The assertion still requires the event to announce exactly the version written in that transaction.
    - A negative case still passes a version that differs from the stored one (stored ± 1), never the stored value.
  - T029 and T030 keep [X], because their modes must still pass.
- **T006 readings (A-010).** These are clarifications, with no contract change, so T006 (2e22b5e, vendored from 7f52d00) needs no re-vendor.
  - (a) Duplicate or gapped recipient ordinals give 400 `invalid_request`. The EnvelopeCreate description is part of the schema, and the BadRequest response covers any schema violation.
  - (b) A malformed `envelope_id` in the path gives 400 `invalid_request`. The EnvelopeId pattern is part of the contract, and every operation with that path parameter declares 400. A malformed ID names no envelope, so 400 leaks nothing; the uniform 404 applies to well-formed IDs. **Re-ruled by request #16:** T031's accepted router gives the uniform 404 `not_found` before auth for any path parameter outside its pattern, and that stands (row T006-EC22).
  - (c) Create sets the envelope version to 1, and each successful upload and prepare adds exactly 1 (data-model.md § Transitions and races, item 1).
  - **Coverage outside T006**, which stays as scoped:
    - 401/403 per route: T034 (EA-03), with T005's validator; the recipient-scheme 401/403 cases belong to T008 and T010.
    - Idempotent replay: T034 (EA-02, SC002-23), with T007's records.
    - Cross-tenant 404: T034 (SC002-07, SC002-08, SC002-26) and T016 (SC002-09). T010's SC002-02 is a cross-envelope 409 on the session-bound routes (request #24).
    - Send confirmation: T034 (SC002-24).
- **`scan-limit` (A-010).** The no-secrets rule that e1be709 adds is ratified. It is a finding like any other: the gate fails, and the report names the file and the rule, never the content. The scan bound is a constant in `scripts/no-secrets.mjs`; no flag or environment variable raises or skips it. NS-13 proves it on a value of 10 MB or more with no stack overflow.
- **parser-limits run time (A-010).** About 48 s with a sandbox per parse needs no ruling. If one test file nears the frozen runner's per-file timeout, split the file inside T033's `files:`; the runner never changes for it.
- **Contract 0.2.3 (A-010).**
  - Drawn strokes use integer fixed-point coordinates 0..10000 instead of [0,1] numbers, and one Approval holds at most 15,000 drawn points (S5). The worst case within the schema serializes to about 217 KiB of compact JSON, under T031's unchanged 256 KiB limit; S5's example was about 508 KiB.
  - The AuditReceipt wording now says "chain head at package assembly" (S4).
  - Owners: the contract (this commit), T009 capture (AG-07), T010 validation (AP-01) and T036 rendering (CP-09).
  - T031 needs no change, so the live post-merge T031 session is unaffected.
  - T006 re-vendors 0.2.3 at its integration after Phase 2 close, before T007.
- **Contract 0.2.2.** Adds `ApprovalManifest.seal_audit_anchor` and `VerificationReport.approval_evidence_digest` (S1), plus the `cropbox_differs` wording (S2).

## Implementation strategy

1. Finish setup and foundation first: the runtime, the gates and the isolated parser.
2. Stop at US1 to demonstrate "fully approved" without sealing.
3. Complete US2 for the signing MVP.
4. Add US3, then the release gates.

## Landing obligation

After implementation, reconcile these spec artifacts against tested behavior, obtain leg reviews and land matching assembly pins. Do not archive because documentation is complete. Any accepted bounded amendment updates the working spec/plan/tasks immediately and uses the shared reconciliation issue protocol. Wider identity, licensing, live ledger or healthcare deployment changes require full governance first.
